When available in an equipped Development checkout, run targeted worker Node tests and worker/db typechecks without waiting for CI/Vercel or requiring DEV-01 gates.


### FIX01-SCHEDULER-CLAIM-STARVATION-20261022 — Filter existing durable runs before batch LIMIT
- **Scope and recovery:** Owner directed FIX-01 only, commits directly to `Rilan-Dev/ai-comms-agent/tree/Development`, no GitHub Actions/Vercel waiting, no feature development, no docs edits except append-only **root `worklog.md`**. Inspected current `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `AGENT_BACKLOG.md`, FIX-01 notes, scheduler worker, DB claim code, prior regression tests and SQL migrations 0021 and 0043. Phase 4 remains `implementation_in_progress`, scheduler reliability assigned FIX-01; DEV-01 and OPS-01 scopes unchanged. Starting Development HEAD `084f36bad0a6c7f1a485bee663e2a68a1f36a8fc`.
- **Symptom and root cause:** After a worker interruption or failed post-run schedule advancement, `scheduled_jobs.next_fire_at` can remain due with an existing `scheduled_job_runs(scheduled_job_id,fire_at)` record (running, completed or failed). Previous `claimDueScheduledJobs` selected the earliest `LIMIT $2` active due jobs **before** attempting idempotent run INSERT. Every already-recorded fire then hit `ON CONFLICT ... DO NOTHING` and was skipped in TypeScript. If the oldest due jobs number at least the batch limit, they can repeatedly consume all candidate slots and indefinitely starve other due schedules without their own run records. The migration 0021 unique partial index enforces one run per fire and 0043 has fallback; those constraints remain correct. This diagnosis is proven from source ordering, not from a live Postgres incident.
- **Test first / RED:** Added focused regression in `apps/worker/tests/scheduling-idempotency.test.ts`, commit `74dc2347f3f5e4782d3da7d407e727d1250fab37`. It requires alias-qualified scheduled-job selection, a correlated `NOT EXISTS` filter against durable runs matching job ID and fire timestamp, filtering before `ORDER BY ... LIMIT`, row-level `FOR UPDATE OF j SKIP LOCKED`, and unchanged SQL `ON CONFLICT` fallback. Comparing the exact starting GitHub file `084f36ba` against this contract gave **1/4 source checks passing** (existing conflict fallback) and **3/4 failing** (missing ahead-of-LIMIT filtering and scoped row lock). The regression explicitly fails against prior source, not due to syntax or import errors.
- **Remedy / GREEN:** Commit `639098b792313db7ed5df5a7acbc909f8f657c83` changes **only** `packages/db/src/automation/repository.ts` claim selection to `SELECT j.* FROM scheduled_jobs j ... AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM scheduled_job_runs r WHERE r.scheduled_job_id=j.id AND r.fire_at=j.next_fire_at) ORDER BY j.next_fire_at ASC LIMIT $2 FOR UPDATE OF j SKIP LOCKED`. The unique INSERT remains a concurrency safety net. Due jobs with an existing durable run are excluded **before limiting** candidates; unrelated jobs can proceed without re-dispatching possibly side-effectful interrupted occurrences. After edit, the same 4 source contracts were **4/4 PASS** against a fresh GitHub Development blob and committed regression was read back. PostgreSQL SELECT/locking documentation confirms `FOR UPDATE OF alias SKIP LOCKED` and `LIMIT` semantics; no SQL execution is claimed.
- **Failed/unsafe approaches avoided:** Do not delete/rewrite the durable unique index, catch all database INSERT failures, blindly retry an unknown-outcome external action, clear `running` records without an explicit lease/recovery/side-effect policy, run production migrations, or disable tenant checks. The filter prevents batch starvation; the interrupted individual schedule still needs carefully designed recovery and possible manual reconciliation, which remains **OPEN**.
- **Verification evidence/limits:** GitHub fetched actual pre-fix and post-fix repository snapshots, structural RED 1/4 vs GREEN 4/4; committed regression source inspected. No private dependency-equipped checkout or disposable PostgreSQL was available, so exact worker Node tests, DB typecheck, transactional concurrency/lock tests, crash-restart tests, CI, Vercel, deployment and runtime delivery are **NOT RUN**. Source contracts do not establish runtime acceptance. No app feature activation or other Markdown edits.
- **Prevention and next action:** Claim candidate queries must exclude unclaimable/idempotent rows ahead of `LIMIT`, preserving fair progress while durable uniqueness protects races. Next FIX-01 task: isolate and reproduce **safe detection/recovery of stale scheduled_job_runs** without duplicating provider side effects; assess proper leases/heartbeats and ambiguous outcomes before changing retry behavior. On a sanitized equipped Development checkout run `cd apps/worker && node --import tsx --test tests/scheduling-idempotency.test.ts tests/scheduler-worker.test.ts tests/cron.test.ts`, root `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/db typecheck` and worker typecheck, plus read-only/disposable PostgreSQL claim fairness concurrency cases. DEV-01 owns features, OPS-01 infra/deploy. No waiting for CI/Vercel.

Missing text or oversize returns NULL HTML preview rather than attempting unsafe regex-based HTML-to-text; existing text/snippet remain. This intentionally loses rich formatting. Shared export `85d332987db2419d162c0710b98d72088d3d840c`, worker single ingress replacement `e22ac5f87cfc204d9f97fd36293399190b735a78`. No new dependency or lockfile churn.

DB repository (`02291ace4a2b0272b9b8427c4eb938b8df48e4e4`) applies this guard on both single-message and thread result rows before returning the fields `html_body_sanitized` and `html_body` to callers; organization and mailbox filters unchanged. ### FIX01-SCHEDULER-INTERRUPTION-RECOVERY-20261022 — Quarantine stale and stranded schedules without replay
- **Role, recovery and authorization:** Owner directs FIX-01 / Sentinel to fix implementation errors directly on `Development`; another AI owns features, OPS-01 owns deployment/infrastructure, no CI/Vercel/gate polling. Recovered current GitHub `Development` start `ac845040cbac95fea72e68460d9f487eee9050cf`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, phase/state and FIX-01 scheduler backlog, root and role worklogs, DB scheduled_job schema/migration 0021, existing scheduler worker/repo/tests, and analogous fail-closed stale Email/WhatsApp recovery. All project-control Markdown read-only. **Root `worklog.md` is the ONLY Markdown file edited by FIX-01.**
- **Confirmed source defect / root cause:** Claim fairness fix `639098b` intentionally excludes durable `scheduled_job_runs` at the same `fire_at` to avoid duplicate side effects. But an interrupted execution can leave `scheduled_job_runs.status='running'` forever and its parent active/due; retries are blocked with no terminal classification, recovery alert or visible remediation. A distinct failure arises after `completeScheduledJobRun` succeeds but advancing `scheduled_jobs.next_fire_at` fails: the run is terminal yet the parent stays active/due, and the prior claim-dedup filter keeps it stranded. Also the previous unconditional run settlement `UPDATE ... WHERE id=$1` permitted a late executor to overwrite a recovered terminal status. No evidence of any particular real user job or provider outcome; these are deterministically source-reproduced lifecycle gaps.
- **Scope/invariant:** Complete the bounded **source-level interruption reconciliation path**, not an automatic resend/retry feature. External product actions may have been performed; never dispatch a known `fire_at` twice based only on a timeout. Preserve user pause, archive, edit and org tenant boundaries, make unknown outcomes explicit in run metadata and schedule error fields, do not drop duplicate-run protection, avoid schema/migration changes, and leave review/resumption under existing authorized schedule APIs.
- **TDD/RED:** Wrote `apps/worker/tests/scheduler-stale-recovery.test.ts` first, direct Development commit `fd5f45361ac477a9c8d44516a15dc1dd37463a82`, checking: bounded age and row locking, fail-closed interrupted result, same-org/same-fire/edit-fenced parent quarantine, recovery-before-new-dispatch and audited manual verification, and compare-and-set terminal settlement. Exact old GitHub source contained **0/5** required integration boundary invariants. Subsequently added a focused test for durable completed/failed runs stranded by a failed parent advance, commit `05015dd4d4f2413ada9bb9f450c14ea79f095c81`, red before terminal reconciliation was authored. These tests are source contracts; they do not replace database concurrency tests.
- **Remedy (4 source commits):** `0dcdf678d69d1fb0ca9d3a07d8628b03a7bf1f06` implements `recoverStaleScheduledJobRuns(nowIso,limit,maxAgeMinutes)` in a single `withTransaction`, using age cap 24 hours by default, at most 100 rows, `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE OF r,j SKIP LOCKED`, same row/tenant/fire and `updated_at <= started_at` fences. Recover stale running runs as terminal failed with `providerOutcomeUnknown:true`, `manualVerificationRequired:true` and error text, and mark an unchanged active parent `status='error'` with visible `last_error`. The run remains immutable against late settlement via `WHERE status='running' RETURNING id` both on complete and fail. `bfa0801a2fc32bf12a1ec4de9fc869033a4e3974` exports recovery from `@ai-comms-agent/db`. `839a00e415a8e082614e34f33eb2d56aad2a89b8` invokes recovery before new scheduled claims, emits best-effort tenant-scoped audit per recovered run, checks compare-and-set result before recording completion/failure and **does not advance a late terminal run**. `79b227fc3b44dbeafe57f4294504a5f38107c3ec` extends bounded reconciliation to stale `completed`/`failed` occurrences whose parent remained at the same due fire: keep terminal run unchanged and quarantine the unchanged active parent for manual review. `05665b161f90c925ba2ccd6f37b7dd42b92f08a1` distinguishes `schedule.execution.interrupted` (unknown provider outcome) from `schedule.progression.interrupted` (terminal but stuck) in audit metadata; never claims known completed delivery was ambiguous. [Note: this sequence contains five source commits: DB initial, index, worker, DB terminal extension, worker audit.]
- **Recovery behavior boundaries:** Stale means `started_at` older than the explicit clock minus default 24-hour age; it is a conservative timeout, **not proof** the worker crashed. The DB records uncertainty rather than replaying. Edited or paused jobs are not reactivated; terminal run rows are never overwritten. Existing schedules UI already renders `status` and `lastError`, so no new UI/workflow was taken from DEV-01. Existing authorized PATCH may review/reset a job, but there is no automated compensation, external-side-effect reconciliation, or assurance that a 24-hour execution is stopped remotely.
- **Verification and limitations:** Re-fetched current Development DB, worker, DB exports and regression blobs. A focused source-contract pass checked **16/16** integration invariants (transaction/row lock/age/limit, unknown outcome JSON, terminal same-fire handling, tenant and updated_at fences, worker ordering, audit type, exports, CAS success/failure and existing duplicate-claim behavior). Source RED was explicitly observed before both implementation slices. **NOT_RUN:** exact native `node --import tsx --test tests/scheduler-stale-recovery.test.ts tests/scheduler-worker.test.ts tests/scheduling-idempotency.test.ts`, full worker suite, DB/worker typechecks, disposable PostgreSQL concurrency/crash/restart with real transactions, deployed worker, provider side effects, CI and Vercel. This environment lacks an equipped private checkout and PostgreSQL; static/source checks are not production acceptance. No database schema, migration, sending privileges, or live provider state changed.
- **Failed/unsafe approaches avoided:** Do not delete run records or unique index, replay an unknown outcome, use a single unconditional UPDATE without CAS, reset schedules edited or paused later, trust event payloads to select another org, silently change recurrence behavior, mark real production operational or close a gate without runtime results. The 24-hour threshold is fixed in code and may need reviewed product-specific lease/heartbeat design if legitimate tasks can exceed it.
- **Prevention lesson and next action:** Completion requires a durable **run terminal state plus fenced parent progression**, and uncertain after-claim crashes need bounded, auditable, no-replay quarantine. In an authorized sanitized dependency-equipped Development checkout execute `cd apps/worker && node --import tsx --test tests/scheduler-stale-recovery.test.ts tests/scheduler-worker.test.ts tests/scheduling-idempotency.test.ts tests/cron.test.ts`; `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/db typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/worker typecheck`; then run disposable PostgreSQL stale running/terminal, late executor CAS, concurrent pause/edit and two-tenant failures. ### FIX01-SCHEDULER-REVISION-FENCE-20261022 — Honor same-fire edits during in-flight executions
- **Context, ownership and constraints:** Follow-up within the same authorized FIX-01 scheduler reliability workstream on `Development`, after the bounded unknown-outcome and terminal-run recovery implementation logged immediately above. Another AI implements new features; OPS-01 owns CI, Vercel, deployment, database migrations and infrastructure. Did not create a branch, PR, activate routines or edit any Markdown file except append-only root `worklog.md`. User requested a completed touched slice rather than cosmetic incremental work.
- **Root cause and complete failure path:** Earlier `advanceScheduledJob` fenced by `id`, trusted `organization_id`, `status='active'` and expected `next_fire_at`. A concurrent authorized PATCH can change name, cron, timezone, intent, params, or other material settings yet calculate exactly the SAME next-fire instant. An old worker then still matches the parent and can overwrite the user's newly configured schedule status/due state, despite the previously added fire/status fence. The run's selected `scheduled_jobs.updated_at` was not carried through the claim and completion boundary, so no revision comparison existed. This is source-reproduced; not evidence of a real customer incident.
- **RED / regression-first:** Updated `apps/worker/tests/scheduling-idempotency.test.ts` in test-first `Development` commit `2ef62cd2f147d3ce5013c80a658e10dd0a3679fe`, adding contract assertions that claim captures a DB `updated_at` revision as ISO, advance SQL matches that exact `updated_at` as well as org/status/fire, all bind positions are correct, and both worker success/error advancement paths pass the trusted claimed revision. Three distinct required pre-fix source conditions were absent (0/3), so tests would fail against the previous repository source rather than merely documenting current behavior.
- **Correction and full route integration:** `8957bf3a8bf4112645021c954d66732d14635a24` adds nonnullable `revisionAt` to `ScheduledJobClaim`, converts PostgreSQL `updated_at` to UTC ISO, and extends `advanceScheduledJob` to require `updated_at=$4::timestamptz` in addition to the existing tenant/status/fire CAS predicates. All next-fire/status/error SQL placeholders and seven bound arguments were updated together. `3fb397da5fd9ff3e2349544728db46d8a2f137d4` propagates `job.revisionAt` through BOTH the success and error callsites. If an API edit changes schedule revision while dispatch runs, affected UPDATE returns zero rows, leaving the user's later schedule untouched rather than reactivating or overwriting it. No new migrations or provider calls.
- **Evidence and limits:** Fresh latest-Development GitHub source snapshots passed 16/16 focused checks over revision capture, ISO timestamp, exact CAS SQL/binds, both callsites, committed tests, stale/terminal recovery, run settlement protection, tenant boundaries, audit distinction, and existing claim fairness. **NOT_RUN**: real `node --import tsx --test` worker suites, DB/worker TypeScript checks, isolated PostgreSQL edit-vs-in-flight transaction races, provider sends, live worker execution, CI/Vercel or production deployment. Source checks do not prove transactional runtime acceptance, and exact timestamp semantics require disposable PostgreSQL validation. If someone deliberately edits while keeping both `next_fire_at` and `updated_at` unchanged (e.g., out-of-band DB writes), the revision fence cannot observe it; all normal API PATCH writes `updated_at=now()`.
- **Prevention / next FIX-01 action:** Treat scheduled execution claims as snapshots with both occurrence identity and schedule revision; never advance by ID or expected fire alone. The touched scheduler **source lifecycle** now includes bounded recovery, no-replay unknown-outcome classification, state/tenant/revision fencing, best-effort outcome audits and test coverage. Do not describe it as Phase 4 acceptance without actual DB races. On an authorized sanitized dependency-equipped Development checkout run `cd apps/worker && node --import tsx --test tests/scheduler-stale-recovery.test.ts tests/scheduling-idempotency.test.ts tests/scheduler-worker.test.ts tests/cron.test.ts` plus `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/db typecheck` and worker typecheck; use disposable PostgreSQL to test paused, edited-same-fire, terminal advance, 24h stale, and late completion. OPS-01 owns deployment/provider and CI/Vercel, DEV-01 owns any expanded pause/review workflow. Continue independent FIX-01 bugs without waiting for gates.

### FIX01-IMAP-KNOWLEDGE-DOUBLE-INDEX-20261022
- **Scope/authority:** FIX-01 / Sentinel; owner reports that although inbox/thread duplication was corrected in `cce92f98/0cc16a77`, IMAP/SMTP Gmail messages are still indexed twice. Work directly on `Development` and preserve concurrent commits. Recovered latest repo HEAD, `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, shared `AGENT_MEMORY.md`, FIX-01 memory/worklog, verification state, email sync/indexer, outbox/vector lifecycle and related source. Phase 4 remains `implementation_in_progress`. Owner's Markdown restriction: append **only lowercase root `worklog.md`**; no other docs changed. No migration/branch/PR/production/credentials/CI/Vercel waiting, and DEV-01 feature ownership preserved.
- **Symptom and source root cause:** IMAP adapter collects the same RFC822 email via `INBOX` and `[Gmail]/All Mail`. Physical `email_messages` rows legitimately have different `provider_message_id=folder:UID` for folder operations. Earlier inbox/thread SQL deduplicated *display* only; `processEmailKnowledgeIndexingBatch` still selected every `email_messages.indexed_at IS NULL` and independently called `indexEmailKnowledge`. Each folder UID produced its own `knowledge_documents.storage_path`, knowledge chunks and `email_knowledge_sync_jobs` vector upsert (hash and point identity are folder-qualified). That creates two retrieval/index identities for one message. Already indexed copies had nonnull `indexed_at` and would not be automatically reconciled by a future pending-only scan.
- **Reproduction/RED:** Confirmed in pre-fix `Development` source: no account+RFC822 Message-ID canonical ownership in the indexer query, no shadow/duplicate guard in `indexEmailKnowledge` or vector `loadCurrentMessage`, and no reconciliation of legacy knowledge documents. Added five focused source-regression tests before implementation in `apps/worker/tests/email-knowledge-folder-dedup.test.ts` (commit `4638b38e`). They require canonical selection, pending duplicate suppression, legacy document/vector cleanup, survivor promotion and physical-row preservation. Live Gmail account indexes and actual Qdrant state not inspected.

## 2026-10-22 — DEV-01 / Forge — Chat and voice email draft approval UX plus mail permission presets
- **Authority / recovery:** Owner explicitly switched to DEV-01 / Forge on `Development`, requesting a reliable AI chat/voice/realtime-voice draft→confirm→send experience, easy bulk permission controls with per-tool fine tuning, and in-chat admin confirmation. Recovered current GitHub head `4be377ec4a7eba7ee2eb258122f0ffa469a8b439`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, shared `AGENT_MEMORY.md`, central `AGENT_EVENTS/SESSIONS/BACKLOG/HANDOFF`, `CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, state/phase/issues/verification, DEV-01 role memory/worklog, chat/voice/realtime/Clara/email runtime and approval API source. Phase 4 implementation-in-progress; C3/routines new worker remains off; FIX-01 owns independent bugs and OPS-01 owns rollout, credentials, provider test and CI/Vercel. No secrets or customer email content recorded here.
- **Reported problem and architecture trace:** Live Sarvam agent reportedly claims to be text-only and merely returns copyable draft prose despite admin mail permissions. Earlier FIX-01 Sarvam adapter commit is on `Development` but **not proven deployed**. Source has authenticated chat tool loop, voice-turn `runEmailAgentTurn`, realtime voice host-turn bridge, registered `email.draft.create` and `email.send.request`, durable `pending_approval` and a separate approval worker. Saved agent tool grants, the current user's `email.write`, provider capability, connected agent-primary mailbox and human send approval all remain required; a policy checkbox is not direct-send authority. The product UX lacked a reliable, user-confirmable compose path independent of how a selected LLM happens to phrase a tool call, one-click tool groups, and permission controls reachable in chat.
- **Implemented reviewed chat compose flow:** Added authenticated `GET/POST /api/chat/mail-compose` requiring an active org user and existing org-owned agent. GET lists only connected, nondeleted, **primary-bound** same-org mailbox identities and reports current `email.write`+persisted registered-tool readiness. POST never sends: validates recipient/subject/body or saved draft ID; re-verifies primary binding, builds identity from the server-authenticated subject (no client-controlled org/agent/membership authority), creates a new `createEmailToolRuntime`, and invokes **existing** audited/schema-checked/allowlist-filtered `email.draft.create` or `email.send.request` via `invokeEmailReadTool`. The request-for-approval `turn_id` is deterministic per agent/saved draft, using existing DB idempotency to avoid duplicate approval rows after a retried HTTP call. Returns only draft/action identifiers and truthful `draft_created`/`pending_approval`; no automatic human approval, worker dispatch or provider API is called. The separate `/mail/approvals` page retains explicit final approval.
- **Chat UI:** Added an editable, staged composer (select assigned mailbox, recipient, subject, message; **Save email draft**, then separately confirm **Request send approval**; review actions via `/mail/approvals`). Editing fields invalidates any previously saved draft ID and disables send-request until saved again. A bounded deterministic parsing helper recognizes the owner-reported `send email to … for test email` and `draft the test email to …` forms as **review-only** open-composer actions; it does not send, grant authority or intercept unrelated prompts. Default editable test message provided, with no fixed recipient or real send.
- **Admin policy UX:** Added registered-only four quick presets (`Read mail`, `Draft mail`, `Request sending`, `No mail tools`), preserving unrelated existing custom grants and retaining individual checkboxes and advanced sender/security/rate-limit settings. Agent Policy selection is preview-only until a confirmation dialog and existing admin-authorized PATCH. Added administrator-only authenticated GET of persisted policy for chat controls; chat displays presets, fine-tuning and explicit `Confirm and save permissions`, posting existing restrictions unchanged (no silent elevation). Chat links to detailed policy tab; policy page accepts `?tab=policy`. Mailbox binding and actor `email.write` are still separate and never granted by the preset itself.
- **Voice and realtime-voice:** Existing `/api/voice/turn` already uses the same host-authorized email tool executor; realtime transport instructs model to delegate transcript requests there rather than taking independent send authority. Tightened `runEmailAgentTurn` to expose **only tools with the trusted actor's actual permission** in voice-model tool definitions (executor still revalidates), with test-first regression. Voice workspace now indicates approval semantics and, when no live session is running, provides a safe link back to agent-specific chat. Chat deep link resolves only an agent in the authenticated organization, never an arbitrary supplied tenant ID.

## FIX01-MAILTO-ENCODED-HEADER-20261022 — #36 rich email HTML sanitizer link boundary, Development, 2026-10-22

- **Authorization and recovery:** Owner said proceed after six-issue evidence search/conditional closure. Recovered current Development aefa20c9b6abc3b66f1fbc155f3acd9ce8b007db and main 2631bbaa7360d188167783e376937721fda5a12c, docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md and NEXT_ACTION.md, root worklog and issue #36 comments. Maintained FIX-01 direct-Development only, no new branch/PR/force, no production migration or secrets. OPS-01 retains historical HTML cleanup, host investigation and production deployment.
- **Independent security defect (RED):** parse5 rich HTML renderer removes external resources, but safeHref() mailto policy accepted percent-encoded line breaks and headers because regex `^mailto:[^@?]+@[^@?]+$` checked canonical URL with no unencoded ? or #. Exact original safeHref logic copied from Development source into isolated local Node v22 produced non-null accepted href for `mailto:friend@example.org%0d%0aSubject:Injected`, `%0aX-Trace:evil`, `%3fsubject%3dhello` and double-encoded variants. Such values can be decoded as email-client headers despite being inert HTML strings; the only supported mailto link contract is simple recipient address, not prefilled control/header injection.
- **Test-first + minimal fix:** Added adversarial `apps/worker/tests/email-html-rich-sanitizer.test.mjs` case in 9285d8fd260add6a704c0017828b5b35813be6ef, checking all encoded header/CRLF variants, multi-recipient comma, Bcc query, ordinary +tag address, verified v2 read. Tightened shared safeHref mailto target to `/^mailto:[a-z0-9._+-]+@[a-z0-9-]+(?:\.[a-z0-9-]+)+$/i` with the prior URL search/hash restrictions unchanged in ab5a5b6b21d2d11ce3c056a3b6d25234672c593e. URL fragment, userinfo and HTTPS existing checks preserved. No CSS, remote images, unsafe schemes, implicit sends or extra HTML attributes introduced.
- **Executed verification/evidence:** Node v22 safeHref isolated function harness 11/11 PASS for eight dangerous URL schemes/headers and three valid email/HTTPS URLs. Refetched committed shared blob 443d2fd530cb615a28fdcad156e5dc39bd07d905, actual regression blob 72a804eec169797e6d31ce22b0ae85449ef8d191 and default worker test registration. Independently located public published package-lock records confirming parse5@7.3.0 and entities@6.0.1 SRI values in Development lock. Exact native parse5+tsx test, pnpm frozen install, shared/db/worker workspace typechecks, real provider ingest/legacy tenant DB/browser, prod/main and at-rest cleanup **NOT_RUN** due unavailable private checkout and dependencies.
- **Issue state:** GitHub #36 updated with proof and remains OPEN. New mailto link fix is Development only, while main still accepts raw provider HTML and has no read quarantine. Needs reviewed promotion and OPS-01 runtime/at-rest security acceptance. Do not close #37/#38/#33/#32/#26 on source-only checks.

### FIX01-SARVAM-AGENT-VOICE-OVERRIDE-20261022 — 2026-10-22
- **Role/authorization:** FIX-01 / Sentinel, owner explicitly requested smallest reproducible source correction on `Development`; Phase 4 `implementation_in_progress`. Recovered latest Development, `CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, shared `AGENT_MEMORY.md`, central sessions/events/handoffs/issues/verification and FIX-01 memory/worklog. OPS-01 owns deployment/environment, DEV-01 new UX and features; no migrations, provider calls, permission changes, new branch or PR.
- **Reported symptom:** Agent AI Settings page shows Sarvam for chat, voice-chat and realtime voice, but user-provided authenticated POST `/api/voice/session` persisted `provider=openai`, `model=gpt-realtime-2.1-mini`, `voice_id=alloy` and `/api/voice/realtime` SDP endpoint; subsequent SDP POST returned 503 `realtime_not_configured` (OpenAI). Sensitive user-pasted `aca-access-token` was not used, repeated or retained; advise revoke/rotate session immediately.
- **Confirmed source root cause:** Agent AI Settings PUT saves per-agent realtime selection under `agent_ai_settings.realtime_voice_provider_override` (and `realtime_stt_model`, `realtime_voice_id_override`). But `apps/web/src/app/api/voice/session/route.ts` historically read `agents.voice_provider`, `agents.voice_model`, `agents.voice_id`, which are stale legacy columns and need not reflect the AI Settings panel. When that old value and org defaults were absent, route fell back to `REALTIME_VOICE_PROVIDER || 'openai'`, selecting OpenAI SDP despite explicitly configured Sarvam. The OpenAI SDP handler then correctly rejected missing OpenAI credentials; it was not proof the Sarvam key was absent. Existing Clara resolver/browser already include Sarvam-specific `/api/voice/sarvam/session` relay and Sarvam speech-pipeline fallback.
- **Remedy:** `ae1f92e0` adds a small pure precedence resolver: saved per-agent AI override wins, NULL modern override inherits org; retired legacy agent voice provider is eligible only when no modern agent AI settings row exists. `505e9e8d` updates `/api/voice/session` to fetch tenant+agent scoped `getAgentAiSettings`, resolve trusted provider, select provider-specific realtime model and saved STT/voice overrides, refuse unsupported provider with 422, then pass the matched settings unchanged to Clara `startRealtimeSession`. Prevents inheriting another provider's org voice model or silently substituting OpenAI. No provider transport/credential/authorization/SDP code changed.
- **Regression:** `13b44a8a` adds seven Vitest cases in `apps/web/tests/voice-agent-provider-selection.test.ts` exercising saved Sarvam vs old OpenAI, explicit NULL inheritance, pre-modern legacy support, unsupported provider rejection, same-org AI Settings route parity, STT/voice isolation and supported Sarvam relay/pipeline dispatch.
- **Evidence:** Re-fetched exact Development GitHub blobs for helper, route, test, Clara, browser and OpenAI guard: **16/16 static/source-level invariants PASS**. These are NOT native Vitest/tsc, database, deployed revision or vendor WebSocket acceptance. Original user HTTP response proves the incorrect OpenAI choice on deployed version, not the Sarvam relay's availability.
- **Failed approaches avoided:** Do not edit saved agent policy or provider selection; do not grant/reuse/require OpenAI credentials to make a Sarvam agent work; do not relax account/tenant checks, mint provider tokens in browser, bypass host tool policy, or route Sarvam SDP to OpenAI. Do not claim native realtime without `VOICE_RELAY_PUBLIC_URL`, `AUTH_SECRET` and a scoped enabled Sarvam provider key; absent relay prerequisites legitimately select Sarvam speech pipeline if its key is accessible.
- **Prevention lesson:** Resolve runtime provider from the same persisted, tenant-scoped contract that the settings UI writes; distinguish NULL=inherit from fallback to retired columns; propagate provider/model/voice consistently across session metadata and native transport endpoint, reject unsupported providers without surprise fallback.
- **Exact next action:** On fresh `Development` run `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web exec vitest run tests/voice-agent-provider-selection.test.ts tests/voice-realtime-transport.test.ts` plus `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web typecheck`. OPS-01 verifies live web/worker source SHA, tenant-scoped saved AI override and Sarvam credential purpose/relay prerequisites without printing secret; after approved deploy, authorized Sarvam-agent `/api/voice/session` must identify `realtimeProvider=sarvam` with `/api/voice/sarvam/session` for realtime or explicitly Sarvam pipeline, never OpenAI SDP. Only when relay WebSocket opens and session transitions to active is native realtime accepted. FIX-01 repairs any newly reproduced source defects; keep existing GitHub #26 open until its broader lifecycle/security acceptance is proved.

## FIX01-CHAT-ASYNC-AGENT-COMPOSE-RACE-20261022 — FIX-01 / Sentinel — Development, 2026-10-22

- **Scope and authority:** User explicitly directs independent error fixing on Development while DEV-01 continues product features; no branch/PR/force, no CI/Vercel waiting, and no Markdown edits other than append-only lowercase root worklog.md. Recovered current Development head 2881e9fdf45f316e2cb8e55011d796e228ef1c56, docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md, NEXT_ACTION.md, FIX-01 role memory, root worklog, latest DEV-01 permission/draft implementation, web chat API/client/test sources and DB chat message projection. Phase 4 remains implementation_in_progress; do not change DEV-01 product intent, agent allowlists, user email.write grants, approval rules or production provider credentials.
- **Real cross-agent context defect:** ChatClient.switchAgent updated agentId BEFORE asynchronous /api/chat/conversation resolved. While new agent header appeared, old conversationId/messages remained attached. Any failed fetch kept new agent selection with old history and emitted a fake system message. Concurrent rapid switches could let an older response overwrite a newer agent. returnFromVoice asynchronously fetched current chat history with no stale-response guard; after another agent switch its older result could reattach previous agent messages. This is user-visible inaccurate agent history and an authorization-context risk if a user submits a new turn during the window.
- **Review/send-approval defect:** ChatClient.openMailComposer and submitMailCompose accepted asynchronous responses unconditionally even after a form edit, different agent or repeated click. Recipient, subject and body inputs stayed editable during pending draft save, so a late saved draft ID could appear for different current text/recipient. Selecting the old draft then requesting approval could present a misleading reviewed payload. Editing did not clear the prior composeActionId and mailbox selection was frozen after saving even when the user needed to revise. The API tools are separate and enforce mailbox/policy/actor grants, but UI must not claim a different saved draft or pending approval than the user reviewed.
- **Regression FIRST (RED):** Commit 355f3569292d93106719a2be2111fcdca603d93b adds apps/web/tests/chat-agent-context-race.test.mjs to assert safe switch, history refresh epoch, composer revision fence and editor invalidation; original code lacked guards. A new Vitest registration file was added later at 251ae6d2. Initial isolated Vitest-callback execution 3/4 due test mistakenly counting Save/Approval button disabled expressions in addition to the four editor fields, corrected in bc0540d5. Native monorepo Vitest invocation unavailable in connector-only runtime.
- **Completed fix:** 9f1416b89631a81bbff3805c1a13eb79ebf154a7 creates a single in-flight switch guard and monotonic view epoch, validates requested agent and response conversation/message IDs, and only adopts agentId/conversationId/messages after validated successful fetch. Failures preserve old context with visible notice. Voice history reloads are discarded when epoch is stale. d2ecd5ab404dc680c910f99e43897ac56c751285 locks async compose setup/submission to a review revision + agent epoch, prevents double-submit before React rerender and requires exact draft_created/pending_approval response shapes before attaching saved IDs; errors cannot yield false approval. fc96a242fc0c0abd0879cd5611562fe02229c49c disables agent switching and form mutation during pending operations, invalidates both draftId and actionId after changes to all four editable fields, clears old review on editor close, and fences message/voice/policy controls. No provider send or approval executed.
- **Executed GREEN against exact GitHub source blob 40481737eebfa40fed839efc95c550ab6dd5ebc3:** Extracted committed switchAgent JavaScript body (only TS type annotations removed) executed in isolated V8 with deferred mocked HTTP and React state: 5/5 PASS covering pending old-history retention, atomic new-agent commit, HTTP failure preservation, mismatched conversation rejection and duplicate-switch refusal. Extracted submitMailCompose body with mocked fetch/state: 5/5 PASS covering stale edited draft response rejection, confirmed draft attaching, invalid status denial, approval double-click idempotence and HTTP-denied approval not marked pending. Executed five committed .mjs source-contract callbacks with mocked Node readFileSync/test/assert: 5/5 PASS after adjusting expectation to require BOTH form revision and agent epoch. Executed four Vitest callbacks with minimal expect shim: 4/4 PASS after bc0540d5, asserting normal web suite requirements. These are isolated source/behavior checks, **not native Node/Vitest, React DOM/browser, TypeScript compiler, DB, provider, production or C3 acceptance**.
- **Files changed / commits:** apps/web/src/app/chat/chat-client.tsx (9f1416b8, d2ecd5ab, fc96a242), apps/web/tests/chat-agent-context-race.test.mjs (355f3569, 9f747d41), apps/web/tests/chat-agent-context-race.test.ts (251ae6d2, bc0540d5), and this root lowercase worklog.md append. All work direct to Development, preserving concurrent commits and avoiding other .md, CI, DB migration, emails, user grant changes and automated sends.
- **Next verification/handoff:** On dependency-equipped Development checkout run Node `node --test apps/web/tests/chat-agent-context-race.test.mjs`, web `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web exec vitest run tests/chat-agent-context-race.test.ts tests/email-permission-presets.test.ts tests/email-write-permission-contract.test.ts`, then `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web typecheck`; verify manual browser deferred network, agent A/B message isolation, failed navigation, editable draft save and separate approval. DEV-01 continues product functionality; OPS-01 owns deployment and runtime provider acceptance. Six unrelated GitHub open issues retain their separate evidence gates; no false closure.

Neither a connected mailbox nor an agent policy preset is a user-level permission grant. Provider draft/send capability is additionally verified by the existing tool executor.

## FIX01-EMAIL-ACTION-IDEMPOTENCY-REVIEW-ISOLATION-20261022 — FIX-01 / Sentinel — Development, 2026-10-22

- **Authority and recovery:** Owner authorized continued FIX-01 source corrections on Development only, independent of DEV-01 feature development. Read canonical docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md and NEXT_ACTION.md plus central AGENT_BACKLOG/AGENT_EVENTS, FIX-01 role memory, root worklog and current head 5fe481d8d390d2aeb6abf0c3bf3df5c2b252b2db. - **Substantial independently reproduced approval-state defect:** packages/db/src/email/actions.ts::createEmailAction accepted retry/idempotency collisions when organization, emailAccountId, actionType, contentHash and draftId matched, but did NOT require the original requested_by actor or agent_id to match. A distinct admin/agent with the same key and same snapshot could be served an existing action requested by someone else. More importantly, after a pending action became approved, completed, rejected or expired, the same key returned the previous action (because DO UPDATE had no status/expiration predicate), yet apps/web/src/app/api/mail/actions/route.ts always reported status:pending_approval with HTTP 201 regardless of actual persisted action.status. The actor-scoped email.send.request tool similarly reported a fresh pending_approval result when reusing a previously finalized action. These defects confuse approval identity/state and can conceal whether a send already occurred.
- **Regression before correction:** Added apps/web/tests/email-action-idempotency-authorization.test.ts at 3950e0673b677c05a3dc2df83921864a6075f341. Cases require pending/unexpired only, same requested_by and agent_id, same account/type/payload/draft, explicit typed conflict returned to host and tool, truthful receipt, unchanged no-automatic-send/human approval/worker transitions. Original code failed identity, state and typed error assertions. Corrected one regression expectation (where the error string lives) in commit 12922ce3299d97c934f201d47e8843ac2f109c28.
- **Completed database and host/tool fix:** packages/db/src/email/actions.ts commit 66394a44a5d5833b9dd6198d4a91a52849c492ec adds EmailActionIdempotencyConflictError and extends ON CONFLICT WHERE with `email_actions.requested_by IS NOT DISTINCT FROM EXCLUDED.requested_by`, same `agent_id`, `email_actions.status = 'pending_approval'`, and unexpired (`expires_at` null or > now). This reuses an existing action ONLY while the same actor+agent+immutable content is still awaiting approval; a denied collision returns no row and throws the typed conflict rather than rearming any action. packages/db/src/index.ts export added 3ce52b1589d27e74077bdded31dfc3ae9846b0a4. apps/web/src/app/api/mail/actions/route.ts commit f3b8d7add1f0cc7fc659edf4e86c2c87227f41d9 returns HTTP 409 email_action_conflict for a rejected retry and binds receipt to action.status rather than inventing pending_approval. packages/email-agent-runtime/src/email-write-tools.ts commit 28efc11f270f19123bca7e6ec6e7e6e06b323d5d preserves separate audited runtime policy/capability/actor gates but surfaces collision as email_action_conflict, not falsely pending. Approval review/decision and worker claim/complete routes unchanged; no provider send or user-grant bypass.
- **Executed verification and limits:** Refetched exact committed source and ran all 3/3 new Vitest assertion callbacks via isolated V8 minimal test shim (not native Vitest). Extracted exact committed createEmailAction TS function and typed error class with only TS syntax erased, injected mock query/hash/row mapper, and executed success+collision behaviors: **10/10 PASS** across org/agent/actor/key binding, SQL predicate presence, pending receipt and typed conflict thrown on zero rows. One exploratory mock initially compared Error constructors from two separate V8 factories, giving a false instance failure; corrected harness to compare each error against its own constructor and reran 10/10 PASS. Did **NOT** run native pnpm Vitest/typecheck, SQL on real PostgreSQL, worker/provider/browser, end-to-end user approval, or deployed verification. No evidence of a real cross-actor incident, only reproducible source-level policy gap; no assumptions about production rollout.

## 2026-10-22 — FIX-01 / Sentinel — Email action execution-clock and terminal-outcome protection

- **Role and recovery:** FIX-01-only source error remediation on owner's `Development` branch (start SHA `5fe481d8d390d2aeb6abf0c3bf3df5c2b252b2db`). Read `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, `AGENT_MEMORY.md`, `AGENT_BACKLOG.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `VERIFICATION_STATE.md`, role-local FIX-01 `MEMORY.md`/`WORKLOG.md`, plus worker/DB action lifecycle, approval tool, draft source and migrations. Phase 4 implementation in progress, DEV-01 independent features and actor-level `email.write` policy decision, OPS-01 CI/deploy/provider/infrastructure. No other Markdown edited, no credentials, migration, branch/PR, gate polling or real provider execution.
- **Root cause / symptom (source-reproduced, NOT confirmed production):** Worker `recoverStaleExecutingEmailActions` used `email_actions.requested_at` to decide whether an action in `executing` is stale. But `requested_at` precedes human approval and actual `claimApprovedEmailAction`; a newly claimed but earlier requested action could be recovered as failed/unknown immediately. Separately `processApprovedEmailActions` put provider delivery + local draft mark + terminal DB update + emitted provider event + audit under one broad try/catch. A draft-mark, success event or audit exception after provider acceptance fell into the provider-failure catch; even if `completeEmailAction("failed")` returned null because a completed row cannot be overwritten, the old catch **still emitted misleading email.action.failed evidence**. A recovered-run audit outage also blocked subsequent approved actions. Unknown external side effects must never auto-replay.
- **Test-first RED:** `apps/worker/tests/email-action-outcome-reliability.test.ts` created in `0b006f36d7f6ff77eb3aa2e143e62b3520e9fba4`, asserting durable claim execution clock, bounded no-replay stale recovery, provider-success terminal completion ahead of draft/audit/event followups, success/failure evidence isolated, error event gated on executing→failed CAS, and recovered-audit outage not blocking future work. Evaluated exact old GitHub source against initial contracts: **0/4 PASS (4/4 RED)**. A later test adjustment `1c8f87c13fb3cdf0ec4894352eabf048bb8c8ce0` corrected assertions for callback syntax and scoped settlement binding, not application logic.
- **Complete smallest-scope fix:** `fbfd7e54f5c2fc042f16d8e930f45511e2e2a0e4` persists `execution_started_at` in already existing `email_actions.result` JSONB as part of **atomic approved→executing** claim. Stale recovery now compares this execution start to timeout and keeps `providerOutcomeUnknown` metadata; prior legacy rows lacking marker fall back to `approved_at` or `requested_at`, with no automatic replay. No migration. `f6c559965dd438d3d2780bdfdcb7354a4c4395a7` isolates the provider attempt from terminal DB settlement. A provider failure emits failed event/audit **only if executing→failed update returned a row**; if DB throws or CAS fails, preserve uncertainty. After confirmed provider acceptance, durable `completed` settlement precedes local draft status mark, success event and audit; each secondary action is independently best effort and logged with action ID/error type only. A DB failure after provider acceptance never fabricates provider failure or retries the external action; prior `executing` row stays for fail-closed stale recovery/manual verification. Recovery audit failure no longer prevents processing unrelated approved actions. Original tenant-scoped account, credentials, approval, unique idempotency and provider registry are unchanged.
- **Evidence / limits:** Fresh GitHub readback of exact committed worker/repository/new regression/prior web action-contract tests yielded **14/14** source contracts PASS (previous four RED became green; clock and legacy fallback, no-replay state, settlement-before-evidence, success/failed conditional emission, best-effort audit/draft and preserved prior contracts). Exact native worker Node tests, pnpm TypeScript, disposable PostgreSQL claim/long-approval concurrency, real provider/DB, browser, deployment, CI and Vercel **NOT RUN** (no dependency-equipped private checkout). Source checks do not establish production acceptance or actual emails drafted/approved/sent.
- **Failed/unsafe approaches avoided:** No use of `requested_at` as new claim lease, no blind replay of unknown provider outcome, no false failed audit after a durable success, no automatic permission grant/email send/approval bypass, no manipulation of production account or leaked session, no change of DEV-01 product work, no awaiting CI/Vercel. This fix does not enable `email.write` for any user; that remains a separately authorized DEV-01 design decision.
- **Prevention/next:** Durable terminal status is authoritative; audit/provider events/local draft bookkeeping are post-terminal, best effort and cannot change result classification. Execution staleness is measured from **execution start**, not request date. In an equipped sanitized current-Development checkout, run `node --import tsx --test apps/worker/tests/email-action-outcome-reliability.test.ts`, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web exec vitest run tests/email-action-contract.test.ts`, then `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/db typecheck && pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/worker typecheck`; disposable PostgreSQL two-worker/older-approval, late completion and failed audit/provider/draft side-effects tests. OPS-01 owns runtime/deployment/SMPP/SMTP credentials and live confirmation. Continue independently fixing real FIX-01 defects without gating on OPS/DEV.

## FIX01-DRAFT-ROW-ACCOUNT-TENANT-ALIGNMENT-20261022 — FIX-01 / Sentinel — Development, 2026-10-22

- **Authority and continuity:** Same owner-authorized FIX-01 direct-Development error-fixing scope, no other .md changes except append-only lowercase root worklog.md. Recovered docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md, NEXT_ACTION.md, FIX-01 state, root worklog, current DB mail draft repository and existing draft/approval tests. No CI/Vercel waiting, new branch, PR, force push, production migration or live provider activity; DEV-01 retains product design and OPS-01 deployment.
- **Real tenant-integrity defect found after first approval-idempotency slice:** `packages/db/src/email/repository.ts::updateLocalDraftForOrganization` trusted `a.organization_id = input organization` and `m.email_account_id = a.id` but omitted `m.organization_id = input organization`, unlike get/list/delete and every downstream provider draft state update. A corrupt/historical cross-tenant `email_messages` row attached to an account from another org could be mutated through a request from the account's org; the draft row itself was not tenant-filtered. Separately `listDraftsPendingProviderSync` queued `m.is_draft` and `syncing` rows joined only by account ID, without asserting `a.organization_id=m.organization_id`, so corrupted cross-tenant rows could enter worker discovery. Tenant consistency must bind BOTH the account and the message row, not rely only on foreign-key association.

All prior downstream transitions kept unchanged.

An isolated execution of the committed TS update body with mocked query revealed malformed `m.id=2` instead of `m.id=$2`. Final robust fix in `809d570f9ca4e85d7b07d7280e2d8580a834fba5` computes `draftIdParam = "$" + (values.length - 1)` and `organizationParam = "$" + values.length`, interpolating these safe numeric parameter tokens into SQL without any user-supplied interpolation. - **Reproduced defect A, after-approval mailbox revocation:** Both `apps/worker/src/email/action-worker.ts` and `apps/worker/src/email/action-executor.ts` loaded a previously approved email action, then fetched its email account and checked only non-null + equal organization ID. `PostgresEmailAccountRepository.getById` excludes soft-deleted accounts, but returns status `pending`, `revoked`, `disconnected`, or `error`. Consequently an approved action could still attempt credential loading and provider dispatch after mailbox disconnection/revocation. Focused source verification 10/10 checks confirmed tests, boundary placement and no send/role mutation inside guard.
- **Reproduced defect B, accepted delivery misclassified failed:** `verifyEmailProviderConnection` invokes the provider call then disconnects inside `finally`. A provider can accept a send/reply/forward but its disconnect can throw AFTER success; the batch worker previously treated this as a failed send and recorded `email.action.failed`, encouraging manual re-send. The direct executor was more vulnerable: it called `markLocalDraftSentForOrganization` inside the provider callback before durable action completion; failure to reconcile a local draft after a successful external send entered its catch path, causing `completeEmailAction(...,"failed")`. Even a database error after a provider accepted call could route to the same false failed handling. These are deterministic control-flow defects, not live provider delivery evidence.
- **Test-first RED and remedy B:** New `apps/worker/tests/approved-email-terminal-outcome.test.ts` `56e6578463737c7157597145aef6b83fd0b7e4ec`, extended at `837385a099ada32a20913202394ab8740d721707`, asserts provider acknowledgement is recorded for send/reply/forward BEFORE connection cleanup, durable completion precedes best-effort draft sync, and accepted external outcomes bypass failed-state settlement. Source `135313f492c6b07ed623547f3a171a67bb3c1c6a` moves direct local draft reconciliation AFTER durable terminal completion, treats its exception as a sanitized best-effort log, and preserves executing/unknown outcome instead of marking failed when provider already acknowledged success. Source `0531d541ea3b7c0b57f2a6111487516c4fceefe3` similarly records `providerAccepted` immediately after each main worker provider send/reply/forward, so a post-send disconnect exception leaves the execution pending fail-closed stale recovery with `manualVerificationRequired:true` instead of marking it failed or inviting replay. Neither executor invokes an unapproved provider action. The previously repaired main worker already records durable completed outcome before secondary draft/audit/event side effects.
- **Verification evidence/limits:** Read back actual GitHub committed helper, worker, executor and regression files. Second lifecycle suite 13/13 structural invariants PASS across both action paths; initial shared guard function executed 8/8 isolated behavior cases. No dependency-equipped private checkout or pnpm available here; `node -v` locally is 22.16.0 but exact native TS test modules, full worker suite, TypeScript checks, disposable PostgreSQL, provider fault injection (disconnect fails AFTER provider response), production account state, approved live send, GitHub Actions or Vercel were **NOT RUN**. Source checks and extracted helper execution are not proof of deployed acceptance. Repo continued advancing; preserve other agent's concurrent commits.
- **Failed/unsafe approaches avoided:** Do not loosen `email.write`, auto-grant owner/admin write, trust the agent checkbox as user RBAC, replay revoked-account actions, delete pending approval evidence, auto-retry ambiguous provider outcomes, mark a sent email failed due to local cache/audit/network cleanup, log credentials or recipient addresses, or access the previously leaked user cookie. Any state modified after initial account validation during an in-flight provider operation remains a timing risk requiring a stronger explicit revocation/lease contract; this correction protects accounts already revoked when the worker starts and post-ack cleanup failures, not a mathematically atomic provider/disconnect transaction.
- **Prevention lesson / next:** Treat user approval as a review gate, not perpetual authority over an account later revoked. At the last dispatch boundary re-validate account tenant and connected state. Once an external provider has acknowledged delivery, persist a terminal result BEFORE optional local reconciliation, and never convert known acceptance to failed due to connection close or secondary persistence. For ambiguous accepted outcome leave durable action executing for bounded stale-recovery/manual verification; no blind re-send. OPS-01 owns deployment/CI/live relay/credentials and permission issuance review remains DEV-01. No real email was drafted, queued or sent in this session.

## FIX01-UNEXPECTED-VOICE-DISCONNECT-HOST-LIFECYCLE-20261022 — FIX-01 / Sentinel — Development, 2026-10-22

- **Scope/recovery:** Owner continues FIX-01 independent code-error remediation on direct `Development` without waiting for GitHub Actions, Vercel or separate phase gates and without interfering with DEV-01 new features. Re-read `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, FIX-01 role memory, root worklog, current voice client and DB session lifecycle. Phase 4 remains implementation_in_progress. GitHub #26 remains open pending live provider acceptance; no other Markdown modified except this append to lowercase root `worklog.md`, no new branch/PR/force, credentials, real WebSocket/provider/production calls.
- **Confirmed realtime lifecycle defect:** `apps/web/src/app/ai-voice/voice-workspace.tsx` already closes microphone/Socket on unplanned Gemini Live or Sarvam/ElevenLabs relay `socket.onclose` or fatal provider event, and setConnected(false) on OpenAI RTCPeerConnection `failed` or `closed`. However these paths did not call the host `DELETE /api/voice/session`, so persisted `voice_sessions.status` could remain active after the actual audio transport died. The active record could continue to authorize `/api/voice/turn` until user manually ended. An async PATCH activate success arriving after teardown could set the client back to connected even though the media had closed. Separately startup catch fired an unverified background DELETE then set local `status:'error'` without confirming the server accepted termination, allowing a fresh UI start despite an orphaned starting/active host session. These are source lifecycle errors independent of provider credentials.
- **Regression before fix:** Added `apps/web/tests/voice-transport-disconnect-lifecycle.test.ts` in `a2057e22035a0ec4f98bf264325eccdeec2b5052`. Test contracts require a single guarded unexpected-disconnect host DELETE, verified matching session/status result before local terminal state, no false host-ended report on HTTP failure, Gemini/Sarvam/ElevenLabs fatal and onclose hooks, irrecoverable OpenAI WebRTC failure hook while preserving transient ICE-disconnected recovery, activation PATCH race fence, and no unverified terminal status for startup failure. Follow-up contracts: `758c6dd6` for startup confirmation, `0fce5b98` for stale session PATCH fence, `0aff36ea` for narrowed payload type.
- **Complete source correction:** `apps/web/src/app/ai-voice/voice-workspace.tsx` `889ea22c24fca163972499a39ebea159497ce039` adds `transportFailureInFlightRef` + `reportUnexpectedTransportLoss`. Captures the affected host session identity, aborts pending audio, stops tracks/sockets, sends tenant-scoped `DELETE /api/voice/session` with reason `transport_lost`, validates HTTP success and returned matching session ID plus status `ended`, and only then updates `sessionRef`, rendered session and transport. On failed/unconfirmed host DELETE the microphone remains stopped, prior status is not falsely changed and UI explains that End session must be retried. Expected local closes and concurrent duplicate failure callbacks don't duplicate cleanup. Relay fatal provider errors and unexpected relay/Gemini socket closure use this routine; OpenAI WebRTC `failed` or unexpected `closed` also uses it, while transient `disconnected` stops claiming transport readiness but is permitted to recover. `patchSession` refuses a late activate before or after HTTP, even when the transport closed during network transit. `854c2499` additionally refuses a PATCH response for an unrelated/replaced session ID.
- **Startup and TS safety:** `428aeeaeae3127d218ad9fc47fbb038646243816` replaces the startup catch's unconfirmed local `status:'error'` with a host-verified DELETE result. Until the host confirms the same ended session, End session remains available and another start is blocked; the user sees accurate recovery instructions. Avoid double DELETE while the unexpected loss cleanup is already in flight. `c26bcdac38f4197bf37c0d632d12bf9bb6b18fbc` narrows a possibly null host JSON payload to an explicit `ended` local variable before session state writes; prevents nullable-response TypeScript uncertainty without weakening host success requirements. Existing user-driven endSession exact ID/status verification remains intact.

### FIX01-EMAIL-MAILBOX-WORKFLOW-PREFERENCE-20261023 — FIX-01 / Sentinel — Development
- **Recovery and authorization:** Owner continues independent FIX-01 error remediation only, direct to `Rilan-Dev/ai-comms-agent` `Development`. Starting current GitHub HEAD `17c19bd2aafce914757e0626d7570f750cae4424`. Read root `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, shared `AGENT_MEMORY.md`, `AGENT_SESSIONS.md`/`AGENT_BACKLOG.md`, FIX-01 `MEMORY.md`/`WORKLOG.md`, `VERIFICATION_STATE.md`, existing multi-mailbox draft readiness/route/UI/tests, tool executor and durable approval behavior. Phase 4 remains implementation in progress. No docs mutation except this append-only **root `worklog.md`**. No new permissions, feature activation, deployment, migrations, provider call, CI/Vercel polling or DEV-01 feature overlap.
- **Root cause and symptom:** With two or more connected, agent-bound primary mailboxes, `GET /api/chat/mail-compose` and the chat UI independently chose the first `canDraft` mailbox, even if it was **draft-only** (`canRequestApproval=false`) and a later mailbox was fully capable of both drafting and requesting send approval. The UI thus disabled `Request send approval` by default, while GET advertised `canRequestApproval=false`, despite a usable account being present. A user could manually select another account, but the application incorrectly chose a degraded workflow as its default and inconsistently implied approval unavailable. This is a deterministic source defect; actual live production with two accounts was not observed.
- **Regression first / RED:** Appended two focused tests to `apps/web/tests/chat-mail-compose-readiness.test.mjs`, commit `dc55fa608c78e3cb339cae8021f475c46e986009`. They require (1) selecting a fully eligible mailbox ahead of a drafts-only earlier one, retaining sensible fallback when no full account exists, and handling zero accounts; (2) shared selection logic used by both GET and chat while preserving per-mailbox readiness on user selection. Before correction, fetched current GitHub sources showed **0/3** required code contracts present (no shared selector, route used `choices.find(choice => choice.canDraft)`, chat used `mailboxes.find(account => account.canDraft === true)`). The new named import would also fail under the prior helper.
- **Complete smallest source remedy:** Commit `6928597bf32eed7c175954f5b25dcd2d627a28bf` exports pure generic `selectPreferredComposeMailbox` in `apps/web/src/lib/mail/compose-readiness.ts` with priority: first `canDraft && canRequestApproval`, else first `canDraft`, else first connected choice/undefined. Commit `8bbb1579b174725655fad56166058378a579ba3f` reuses the selector for the mail-compose GET preferred account and therefore its existing top-level `canDraft`/`canRequestApproval` and blockers; `c3ade03a685f953b9e24d04a643a223868e0b1cb` reuses it in chat's initial account review. User-selected mailbox changes still re-evaluate its own availability and blockers. All independent checks for actor `email.write`, saved agent tool allowlist, agent/tenant binding, provider capabilities, schema validation, two-step draft → send-approval request and human confirmation remain intact.
- **GREEN evidence:** Fetched exact latest GitHub source/test blobs; executed the committed generic selector's **exact function body** as JavaScript (only TS signature omitted) inside an isolated V8 harness: **6/6** scenarios pass (draft-only before full, full before draft-only, only draft account, only blocked account, no full with approval-only oddity, and zero). Checked **8/8** structural source contracts across route/UI reuse, user account switch behavior, separated actions and preserved authorization/tenant constraints. This is **not** native project Node/Vitest, web TypeScript compilation, PostgreSQL account data, browser acceptance, production deployment or provider sending, none of which were performed in this execution environment. No live request or email action.
- **Failed approaches and prevention:** Do not grant `email.write` by default to fix the owner's separately reported disabled buttons: current fixed system-role RBAC intentionally has no such grant, which remains a separate approved permission-design/OPS review. Do not select a mailbox merely because it can draft if the intended staged workflow also requires send approval; avoid drift between API preferred-account summary and UI initial account. Never bypass provider `send` capability, human approval or tool-executor checks. No assumption that a successful GET created any draft.
- **Next action:** In a dependency-equipped sanitized current-Development checkout run `node --experimental-strip-types --test apps/web/tests/chat-mail-compose-readiness.test.mjs apps/web/tests/chat-mail-compose.test.mjs`, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web typecheck` and targeted web Vitest. Exercise an authenticated A/B test with two connected primary mailboxes (first drafts-only, second fully capable), verify GET top-level choice and chat default/explicit selection, ensure POST draft persists and approval stays `pending_approval` until human approval. DEV-01/project authorization still required for least-privilege actual `email.write` user grant; OPS-01 owns live credential/account data, deployed SHA, CI/Vercel, migrations/provider behavior, secret-session revocation and end-to-end delivery. ## FIX01-PROVIDER-DRAFT-SYNC-EXPLICIT-QUEUE-20261023 — FIX-01 / Sentinel — Development, 2026-10-23

- **Authorization/continuity:** User requested substantial independent code error fixing directly on `Rilan-Dev/ai-comms-agent/tree/Development`, no waiting for CI, Vercel or stage gates, concurrent DEV-01 feature changes preserved. Recovered GitHub head `17c19bd2aafce914757e0626d7570f750cae4424`, read canonical `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, central `AGENT_BACKLOG.md`, FIX-01 memory and root worklog. Phase 4 remains implementation_in_progress. No .md edits beyond this append-only lowercase root worklog.md, no branch/PR/force, secrets, production provider calls or migrations applied.
- **Proven state-machine defect and RED:** `supabase/migrations/0015_email_draft_provider_sync.sql` restricts `email_messages.provider_draft_sync_status` to `'local_only','syncing','synced','error'` (DEFAULT local_only), while `packages/db/src/email/repository.ts::updateLocalDraftForOrganization` wrote invalid `'pending'` on every changed local draft, causing PostgreSQL constraint failures for ordinary edits. Worker discovery queried rows already `'syncing'` as if they were queued, and `markDraftProviderSyncingForOrganization` stamped `'syncing'` indiscriminately; two workers could update the same remote draft concurrently, a stale provider result could mark a locally re-edited draft `'synced'`, and a crashed attempt could remain stuck as `'syncing'`. Existing manual endpoint/UI labeled queued requests as already syncing. All source defects were observed in committed SQL/TypeScript; no assertion about actual user/provider failure without deployment evidence.
- **Test-first migration:** Added `apps/worker/tests/provider-draft-queue-safety.test.ts` before implementation at `442127d7fc0172abf8061b5c6d9d9dc9e33746a4`, checking status constraint, tenant/approval gates, atomic single-worker claim, per-attempt fencing, stale recovery, worker and UI consistency. Added additive migration `supabase/migrations/0054_email_provider_draft_queue.sql` at `8e6652f8285e6169c6dc5b9e617353849d7967e8` to permit explicit queued state, add nullable UUID provider_draft_sync_token and partial queue index. Old stale syncing rows older than 30 minutes are moved to error, requiring operator/user inspection; migration NOT APPLIED here. This schema migration must be deployed alongside corresponding code by OPS-01.
- **Complete repository state correction:** `packages/db/src/email/repository.ts` commit `19a2e0900f5bf39b8e40d31178b0388a1c97a7ae` replaces impossible pending write with allowed local_only + clear token on edit; explicit `queueLocalDraftProviderSyncForOrganization` only queues from local_only/synced/error when connected and draft is not bound to pending/approved/executing send approval. Provider queue discovery selects queued, tenant-matched connected accounts. Separate `claimQueuedProviderDraftSyncForOrganization` performs atomic `queued->syncing` UPDATE, issue UUID token and return CURRENT draft content, with same tenant/approval checks; only one racing claimant can win. `markDraftProviderSyncedForOrganization` and `markDraftProviderSyncErrorForOrganization` require matching status syncing and UUID token and clear token when settling, so edits reset token and stale responses cannot stamp a newer draft as synced/failed. `recoverStaleProviderDraftSyncs` moves stale syncing rows to review-required error but never auto-replays an unknown external provider modification. New symbols exported in DB index commit `2d26feed8323148fa5ef4a0911bb5a399dd13f2f`.
- **Complete worker and route/UI integration:** `apps/worker/src/email/draft-sync.ts` commit `07339b348f5a9bf5966752ffc69429ce3cad23ff` atomically claims one queued draft before decrypt/provider work, rejects disconnected mailbox after claim, processes the claimed snapshot, passes lease to terminal SQL, returns skipped on lost edit race, and makes audit-event errors non-terminal once durable sync succeeds; provider errors settle with the matching token and no stale overwrite. `apps/worker/src/email/provider-draft-worker.ts` commit `eb646b4b6bbe72b1a77881d48854e313af5f0fd7` performs stale-attempt recovery before scanning queued jobs. `apps/web/src/app/api/mail/drafts/sync/route.ts` commit `8a654e30d90098c8a9065647c7ec21d599eb9a26` queues only a connected mailbox and rejects duplicate/in-flight/approval-locked requests via scoped HTTP409, returning status queued. `apps/web/src/app/mail/drafts/drafts-client.tsx` commit `6cb91b520d76cc9fbd826fc0e8cdb2392145ba9f` distinguishes queued from syncing and disables duplicate requests.
- **Regression/compatibility correction:** Updated older web tenant SQL test `apps/web/tests/email-draft-tenant-sql-integrity.test.ts` in `1c08915b2bc59015bdd3ecbdd1292a672256f0ad` for the new queue/claim function names and actual queued status, retaining all existing cross-tenant predicates. Added coverage for stale attempts/connected mailbox in worker test `93d7e536df2826298ca9cd314517d66b6e1db398`. No stale preexisting test assumes invalid pending/syncing queue after these updates. No change to automatic email sending, human approvals or unrelated product features.

### FIX01-STANDALONE-MAIL-DRAFT-WRITE-RBAC-20261023 — direct draft and provider sync authorization parity
- **Role/recovery:** Owner instructed FIX-01 / Sentinel to continue independently on `Rilan-Dev/ai-comms-agent` `Development` without CI/Vercel/gate polling and without touching any Markdown except append-only root `worklog.md`. Recovered current initial HEAD `8a654e30d90098c8a9065647c7ec21d599eb9a26`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, shared `AGENT_MEMORY.md`, central agent events, issue/verification state, FIX-01 role-local memory/worklog, and current email compose/draft/approval/identity/tool-executor sources read-only. Phase 4 `implementation_in_progress`. DEV-01 retains product work and role-permission grant design; OPS-01 owns deployment and environments. Never used the previously exposed session token, provider API or production data.
- **Symptom and independent root cause:** The earlier owner's email draft requests were blocked because `GET /api/chat/mail-compose` correctly requires authenticated actor `email.write`, saved agent draft/send-request permissions and a connected capable agent-bound mailbox. While tracing the entire draft workflow, found a distinct **server-side authorization bypass** in `apps/web/src/app/api/mail/drafts/route.ts`: `POST` create, `PATCH` edit and `DELETE` remove drafts all used only `authorize(..., {role:"member"})`, although members have `email.read` but deliberately no `email.write`. Thus the separate standalone drafts endpoints could mutate organization mailbox state without the independent user-level `email.write` check the agent tool executor enforces. `GET` used the same imprecise role check instead of `email.read`. Further tracing directory `apps/web/src/app/api/mail/drafts/` found `sync/route.ts`: its provider draft queue `POST` also used membership-only checking, so it could request an external provider mutation without email.write. This is **source-confirmed**, not evidence the affected operation was ever invoked in production.
- **Full touched surface and RED first:** Enumerated draft subroutes; two routes cover all five operations (`GET`, create/update/delete and provider-sync queue). Added `apps/web/tests/mail-drafts-write-authorization.test.mjs` **before source edits**, commit `13d077cd3e44b2eed786a24b895e6896281f1239`. It requires `email.write` for all draft mutators, `email.read` for standalone listing, no membership-only write guard, authorization before DB mutation, unchanged no-auto-grant role map, tenant/capability checks and source-authorized audit. Fetched exact baseline HEAD `8a654e30`: **0/4 draft method requirements and 0/1 provider-sync write requirements** satisfied (all were member-only). Extended same regression with provider sync coverage `82452c003ae04778b1095462520a2bdc30b1448a` before changing its source.
- **Remedy and existing contract repair:** `d24660f4ee92b71412caf2fbb2b082a53cfe3b27` replaces the four legacy membership-only checks in `apps/web/src/app/api/mail/drafts/route.ts` with `permission:"email.write"` for POST/PATCH/DELETE and `permission:"email.read"` for GET, preserving existing org-scoped repository queries and audit. `c15406148c69d87f4c3f660bffe9a8b79c601cba` applies the same `email.write` guard to the separately reviewed provider sync queue endpoint; same-tenant account, connected status, provider `drafts` capability, atomic repository queue lock and audit remain unchanged. `2c0bc5c8c6c13f24280724c56795bbe124e9369e` updates preexisting `apps/web/tests/mail-draft-action-contract.test.ts`, which incorrectly expected `role:"member"`, to explicitly require the new narrower read/write rights and deny the old guard.
- **Evidence:** Re-fetched exact old and new GitHub source and inspected each handler. RED: **0/5** explicit-operation permission checks before source changes (create/update/list/delete/provider sync). GREEN: **5/5** explicit-operation checks and ordering, with existing tenant, auditing and provider capability invariants preserved. Focused current GitHub checks also confirm `packages/shared/src/identity/types.ts` `SYSTEM_ROLE_PERMISSIONS` does **not** grant `email.write` to owner/admin/member/viewer and the agent tool executor still verifies `context.auth_subject.permissions.includes(tool.required_permission)`. The regression test file is committed, but **native Node test execution, web Vitest, installed dependency TypeScript checks, browser, PostgreSQL/tenant negative tests, real provider sync, CI, Vercel and deployment NOT RUN**. No actual draft, approval, send or external action occurred. Static source parity alone is not runtime acceptance.
- **Failed/unsafe approaches avoided:** Did not add `email.write` globally to existing system roles or forge an actor grant from saved agent permissions; did not bypass authorization via the standalone API or disable existing draft audit, tenant checks, approval locks or capability checks; did not create mailboxes, run migrations, change live policies or wait for OPS-01. Note that because the existing role map intentionally grants **no** `email.write`, the standalone draft mutators now **correctly deny** all default-role actors until DEV-01/project owner explicitly designs and authorizes an eligible actor grant; this source correction alone does not restore drafting to the current user.
- **Prevention lesson:** Authorizing membership or org-read is not authorizing mailbox writes. Every route capable of modifying local drafts or queueing remote provider drafts must enforce the same independently granted actor `email.write`, in addition to tenant-scoped account and provider capability checks. Read-only listing requires `email.read`; the agent policy allowlist and separate human send approval remain independent requirements.

DEV-01/project controller must separately authorize a least-privilege `email.write` grant mechanism (not a hidden role elevation); OPS-01 verifies deployed SHA/session revocation and approved provider integration. Continue independent FIX-01 defects without waiting for CI, builds or unrelated feature gates.
