### FIX01-CONTEXTUAL-CHAT-NEXT-ACTIONS-20261023 — unsolicited actions under every AI reply

- **Symptom / user-provided browser evidence:** In live Chat, the latest assistant answer to a WhatsApp send request was an inability-to-send explanation that merely mentioned "email" as an alternative. Nevertheless the UI appended "Choose a next step" with "Find important mail / Make an action checklist / Plan an email reply" and unsolicited email draft and send-approval buttons. The owner reports this panel appears throughout unrelated interactive AI conversations. The selected live page text was inspected read-only; no requests or messages were sent.
- **Recovery / authority:** Continued source-only FIX-01 error remediation on `Development`, initial SHA `d78e421d4c93c5e090ad7c5c516821669e35e7ca`; read mandatory `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md` and `NEXT_ACTION.md`, current ChatClient, `interactive-message-controls.tsx`, `interactive-replies.ts`, related native tests and live tab. DEV-01 owns feature expansion; OPS-01 owns deployment/provider/infrastructure. Preserve all Markdown except append-only root `worklog.md`.
- **Root cause:** `SuggestedNextActions` called `suggestedChatNextActions(answer)` on the **latest assistant message regardless of request or result**. That helper returned a nonempty generic 3-option fallback even for simple greetings, failures and refused operations. `hasInteractiveEmailActions(answer)` only searched the first 5,000 characters of untrusted assistant prose for email/mailbox/draft-related keywords; a WhatsApp refusal mentioning email qualified, so unrelated email actions appeared. Current `ChatClient` already limited the widget to the latest assistant turn, but that was insufficient. Prior intent-only keyword classifiers and rendering a generic fallback are explicitly rejected here; this does not indicate a failure in the backend authorized host tool executor.
- **Regression-first correction:** `3ed83d2f7b9ab60e2c2eb8f9d3e5c74570362b0b` adds 5-case native Node regression `apps/web/tests/chat-next-action-relevance.test.mjs`: verbatim incident category (WhatsApp refusal), greetings, tool denials/no data, substantive grounded mail summary and deliberate Subject/Body email draft, plus React source wiring. `132d9ebfd3f9e8b1de1e884e74a7d171ce3b2572` adjusts old deterministic suggestions tests to expect no generic fallback. `a510549e8a17399c6f2f6a024c375640dffc290f` scopes pure selectors to both **prior user intent** and **assistant result**, suppresses WhatsApp/SMS contexts and denied/empty replies, requires substantial email-summary evidence before mail follow-ups, and requires an actual structured draft plus explicit email-drafting intent before offering reviewed email actions. `6895e38eb3d137caebcd238f5599d87b3d5a2a78` changes the component to return `null` for no relevant action, render the next-step fieldset only when there are relevant next-step suggestions, and render draft/approval controls separately only when valid. `eb785a990225c95f7380bf128b89712733df39dc` threads the latest user request through ChatClient → MessageBubble → SuggestedNextActions. `07141186c6dd43af5a6b1ca39dac57f2f58ec958` updates the earlier mail-workflow contract. `876281ce5a47220d78f464d3ac840ef695a3d8d3` preserves concurrent regression coverage for short counts, model failures and detailed summaries and reconciles its old helper assertions with the new intent+evidence behavior. `e061cd8093bf220264489515a9109bc8f75d1f6e` retains useful substantive unread-count report follow-ups while omitting unhelpful bare counts. No new tool or network call, no auto-send, no approval bypass, no external side effects, no feature rollout.
- **Evidence / limitations:** Re-fetched current committed sources/tests in GitHub, **17/17 structural invariants PASS**: no generic three-option fallback, no WhatsApp/email keyword false positive, guards for denied answers, substantive mail-summary condition, explicit draft intent/Subject/Body, proper latest-request binding, hooks before conditional return, absence of empty fieldsets, no new network transports, and updated five-case regression plus existing contracts. Native committed Node tests, full web Next build/typecheck, authenticated browser post-deploy, and CI/Vercel **not run in this connector-only environment**; current browser still shows the old deployed code until an authorized OPS-01 rollout. This change affects presentation; it cannot fix a provider lacking WhatsApp tool support or grant email.write.
- **Prevention lesson:** Recommendations must not be inferred from the assistant's topic vocabulary alone. Never show generic decision forms when no decision is needed; the user request plus a meaningful assistant outcome must both justify the affordance. Keep draft/send-review UI separate from read-only suggestion chips, show it only for explicit draft intent, and keep all actions behind user click and the host's normal saved policy, actor permissions, mailbox bindings and human approval.
- **Next exact action:** On up-to-date `Development`, run `node --experimental-strip-types --test apps/web/tests/chat-next-action-relevance.test.mjs apps/web/tests/chat-interactive-responses.test.mjs apps/web/tests/chat-interactive-mail-workflow.test.mjs`, then `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web typecheck` and focused chat UI Vitest. In an authenticated disposable browser, reproduce the WhatsApp request and ordinary greeting: both must show just AI text without next-step/mail cards; verify substantive requested mail summary offers contextual text-only choices, an explicitly prepared email draft offers only a reviewed mail flow, and no button silently executes tools. Record exact SHA and browser evidence; OPS-01 owns deployment. If new reproducible source errors arise, FIX-01 repairs without waiting on CI/Vercel.

### FIX01-CHAT-HISTORY-ON-DEMAND-20261023 — hide unrelated conversation list until requested

- **Symptom and evidence:** User reported the interactive Chat UI showing *all other conversations all the time* alongside the active chat. Read-only inspected the user's selected authenticated Chat tab, which visibly contained a "Conversations" column with several history links next to one ongoing conversation. GitHub `Development` Chat client already initialized `chatHistoryOpen=false` and had a mobile "Chats" control, but `<aside>` carried responsive `lg:flex` and `lg:relative`, forcing its conversation list open in every desktop chat even after the user had not asked for history.
- **Root cause / constraints:** The mobile-only toggle `lg:hidden` and desktop `lg:flex` overrode the closed history state on wide viewports. The permanent 14–16rem sidebar also took space away from the current AI answer. This is *presentation and disclosure minimization*, not tenant/user-level access control: existing history API is still organization+agent scoped and per-user privacy needs a separate reviewed authorization/ownership design. Do not delete old conversations or silently change the database/role model. Owner requests that previous chat links be displayed **only on demand**, not in every chat.
- **RED / test-first:** `dd28374c8c5d3b624f75e8492b750db0031af9aa` added four `apps/web/tests/chat-history-on-demand.test.mjs` native Node source-contract cases for closed default at every viewport, desktop-accessible Chats button, Escape/outside/Close and keyboard focus semantics, preserved current conversation and unsent-draft navigation safeguards. A later test-precision correction `c025663766468e8b279e7ba6a207513a04debc25` checks the actual conditional draft confirmation in recent-chat links. The old current `lg:flex` condition violated those conditions before implementation; full RED runner not executed.
- **Full minimal UI correction:** `ff0a88498ac6facf10025ee9a6cffeb3922f74c9` removed desktop unconditional history rendering and mobile-only Chats/Close visibility, retained `chatHistoryOpen=false` by default, and made the conversation list a temporary overlay panel across viewport sizes. Outside-tap backdrop and Close dismiss it; Escape closes; keyboard focus remains trapped in the modal on **desktop and mobile** and returns to the Chats button on close. The active chat gets available width, while older chats can still be opened intentionally through original tenant/agent/conversation links and existing unsent-draft confirmation. No conversation record, tool permission, mail/voice functionality, routing, feature flag, provider, deployment or schema changed. `53ccee6a484649db3b74267f9b168214c458d302` updated existing `chat-conversation-sidebar-layout.test.ts` to enforce on-demand layout instead of legacy permanent desktop panel.
- **Validation:** Re-fetched latest GitHub source, current native regression and existing Vitest contract after commits; **18/18 structural source assertions pass** for default hidden state, no `lg:flex` override, desktop button availability, overlay geometry, click-outside/Close/Escape, desktop-inclusive focus trap/focus return, persistent history links and draft safeguards. **Not run**: committed native Node runner, web Vitest, full typecheck/build, authenticated browser interaction, screen reader/mobile or deployed SHA (this tool has read-only tab access and no dependency-equipped repo checkout). The current live UI may remain unchanged until OPS-01 deploys the amended `Development` revision; do not claim production acceptance.
- **Failed approach/lesson:** Merely storing `chatHistoryOpen=false` cannot hide a pane when responsive CSS forces display at `lg`. Desktop + mobile need the **same state-gated visual contract**. Avoid reducing privacy to a CSS change: true separation of *different users'* histories would require explicit per-user ownership rules, authorization changes and migration/legacy privacy review; not part of this focused UI correction.
- **Next exact action:** In a sanitized current `Development` checkout run `node --test apps/web/tests/chat-history-on-demand.test.mjs`, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web exec vitest run tests/chat-conversation-sidebar-layout.test.ts tests/chat-conversation-history.test.ts tests/chat-turn-navigation-race.test.ts`, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web typecheck` and web build. OPS-01 to validate a deployed authorized desktop/mobile Chat: list absent by default across new and old conversations, Chats opens only when requested, keyboard Tab/Escape/Close/outside dismiss work, one current answer remains visible, list resume/new-chat/agent switching/voice and unsent-draft confirmation unchanged. Separately triage any evidence of real cross-user unauthorized access rather than treating hiding as an access-control fix. Do not poll CI/Vercel or enable gated features.


### FIX01-SARVAM-VOICE-MODEL-CATALOG-20261023 — documented TTS/STT choices

- **Owner/authority:** Continuing FIX-01 / Sentinel source-error role, direct to owner-selected `Development` (initial GitHub tip `3ba9de469e0b6bd225518fd08c1b054f02a938a2`). Read current `AGENTS.md`, `docs/project-control/CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, shared `AGENT_MEMORY.md`, FIX-01 role memory/worklog read-only and the model-catalog, provider setup, endpoint, persisted config and voice speech implementation. Used read-only Browser tab context for the reported AI Provider Setup page; **did not access or change saved API keys, org configs, browser forms or production records**. Root `worklog.md` is the only Markdown file modified by FIX-01.
- **Observed symptom (production UI):** Sarvam Voice pipeline lists TTS and STT credential rows as saved/enabled but marks both `Model not selected`. Both empty select elements display `This provider does not publish a live model listing for this capability. No static model list is substituted. Retry`; setup reports 0/2 capability-ready. This UI is real observed browser context, NOT proof of usable provider credentials, successful decryption, working speech, backend deployed revision or per-key capability access.
- **Root cause:** `apps/web/src/lib/ai/live-model-catalog.ts` rightly restricts Sarvam `supportsLiveCatalog` to **chat**, since Sarvam `GET /v2/models` is a chat-model catalogue. But `GET /api/settings/ai-models` treated every unsupported live purpose as fatal *before* inspecting saved, enabled org-purpose credentials. Therefore Sarvam `tts` and `stt` were always 422 `catalog_unsupported` even after a key was saved, while Voice Chat UI offered no alternative. The same code already supported truthful **vendor-documented** options for streaming realtime voice. Verified current vendor docs `https://docs.sarvam.ai/api/getting-started/models/bulbul`, `https://docs.sarvam.ai/api/getting-started/models/saaras` and the separately scoped chat `https://docs.sarvam.ai/api-reference/chat/models-v2`.
- **Runtime compatibility constraint:** Existing `apps/web/src/app/api/voice/speech/speak/route.ts` actually sends Sarvam `model:"bulbul:v3"`, and `voice/speech/transcribe/route.ts` sends `model:"saaras:v4"`. Sarvam also documents other/later speech models, but this app does **not** yet dynamically choose different TTS voices/request semantics or STT model IDs from the saved setting. Expose **only** these two wired IDs, not advertised-but-unimplemented Bulbul v4 Flash, v2 or alternate STT models. Preserving this constraint avoids displaying configurations that silently execute a different model.
- **Test first / RED:** First commit `4344127f36fd3b2c8cfcee8f5c0399c5ebed8373` extends `apps/web/tests/live-model-catalog-standalone.test.mjs` to require TTS `bulbul:v3`, STT `saaras:v4`, and no cross-purpose or other-vendor choices. The required helper did not exist in starting source and the earlier API returned the unsupported error, independently reproducing the cause by source. Added `apps/web/tests/sarvam-voice-setup-models.test.mjs`, commit `b41d2807dcf5b0b4d897720dbf13cc5b6b2609e6`, protecting organization authentication, enabled decrypted purpose-scoped credential gate BEFORE option disclosure, accurately labeled documented options, no cross-provider ability and rejection of unsupported selections. Vitest guard was extended in `425edfab01e890326857a88c18b7099d18d8ef64`.
- **Correction (five source commits):** `7a249e6a39662e1261fb06f4838c2e53b43affae` adds pure `hasDocumentedSarvamVoiceModels` / `documentedSarvamVoiceModels`, with only the two voice IDs already invoked by the host pipeline. `0abbe3ad8fa4e079ccabef596662232eef9dcf71` allows Sarvam TTS/STT documented path in model GET, **only after** authenticated organization membership and an enabled, decryptable key for exactly that purpose, `source:"documented"` and `capability:purpose` response with no credential disclosure. `f0d512f5b8fb22e4a67c2ecec02d4fadf4b56295` permits this documented capability through the guarded model selector and labels it as vendor documentation, not per-key live availability. `f632f81736f4dbb6157d8b8cb9c16f5d1f5ba229` prevents saving an unsupported Sarvam speech model while preserving the existing credential and realtime policy. `21ff23d329c2a9c77521ae77d1b0c7fdf5ca68ba` corrects voice-setup instructions: Sarvam chat catalog differs from TTS/STT; separately save each purpose's key+model and verify runtime access on use. No other providers' live catalogues changed.
- **Evidence:** Fetched exact current GitHub source, tests and actual app speech requests; **14/14** focused source contracts PASS: org-auth and credential gate, no environment/global API keys, model ID alignment with speech invocations, guarded UI capability source, typed save validation, realtime path and user copy. Ran a JavaScript VM harness executing the **committed helper declarations extracted from GitHub source**, stripping only TypeScript annotations: **6/6** behavior cases PASS for Sarvam TTS/STT and denial of chat/realtime/ElevenLabs/OpenAI cross-purpose use. This is NOT a native repo/TypeScript/Vitest run. Native `node --experimental-strip-types --test apps/web/tests/live-model-catalog-standalone.test.mjs apps/web/tests/sarvam-voice-setup-models.test.mjs`, web Vitest, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web typecheck`, authenticated browser after deploy, live Sarvam API/key and actual audio have **NOT RUN**. No keys read, credential saved, browser form submitted, provider request or production deployment executed.

### FIX01-INBOX-PAGINATION-SQL-BIND-08P01-20261023 — FIX-01 / Sentinel — Inbox SQL parameter binding failure

- **Owner/task:** The user supplied current `web-1` logs reporting `[authorization] unexpected error {sqlstate:'08P01'}`, repeated `db.query` `bind message supplies 4 parameters, but prepared statement "" requires 2` (account + pagination) and `3 parameters ... requires 1` (pagination without account). `/mail/inbox` SSR also failed. Started from current `Development` HEAD `21ff23d329c2a9c77521ae77d1b0c7fdf5ca68ba`, read shared `AGENT_MEMORY.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CHAT_CONTINUITY.md`, `NEXT_ACTION.md`, FIX-01 memory/worklog and exact inbox DB/route/test sources. Source error fix only; do not edit any other Markdown, take DEV-01 features or OPS-01 deployment, or wait on CI/Vercel.
- **Root cause:** `packages/db/src/email/repository.ts` `listInboxMessages` correctly builds a parameter array `[organizationId, accountId?, query?, limit, offset]` and binds account/search filters as `$n`. But its final SQL uses `LIMIT ${values.length - 1} OFFSET ${values.length}` (literal numeric values **2, 3, 4, ...** in SQL) rather than `LIMIT $${values.length - 1} OFFSET $${values.length}` (PostgreSQL parameter placeholders). For filtered account page SQL, PostgreSQL sees `$1,$2` and 4 bound args; for unfiltered SQL it sees only `$1` and 3 args. `08P01 exec_bind_message` therefore reproduces for default Inbox SSR and API pages, not because of the user's authentication. Retain tenant/account/folder restrictions, pagination cap 500, parameterized text search, and sorted page semantics.
- **Regression-first:** Committed `apps/web/tests/mail-inbox-parameter-binding.test.mjs` in `c6ab03250bf9094f0a16cecc585dfacb261b10b3`. The native Node source test extracts the REAL `listInboxMessages` TypeScript function, removes only the signature/one array annotation/one generic type reference, injects a recording `query` stub, and verifies every SQL placeholder from `$1` through the supplied values' highest ordinal, `LIMIT $n OFFSET $m`, all tenant/Inbox guards, account and search value binding, and default/specified pages. It covers unfiltered, account-only, query-only, combined account+query+flags and default page plus a parameterized attacker-string/cap test. No database or provider network needed.
- **RED evidence:** Executed an isolated runtime extraction of actual pre-fix GitHub blob `2a01859b332dfc2da28849cd4ad3264c45f65d96` in a JavaScript V8 harness with recording query callback. All four tested combinations **FAILED (0/4)**: unfiltered 3 arguments vs placeholders [1]; account 4 vs [1,2]; query 4 vs [1,2]; combined 5 vs [1,2,3]. This reproduces the user-visible SQLSTATE problem without credentials, real DB, or changing data.
- **Failed approach:** Initial attempted source edit commit `e2256e8b572bb108a4be8ea7b26055a201a779fd` was a **no-op**: JavaScript `String.replace(old, "LIMIT $...")` treats `$$` in the replacement string as an escape and emitted the original `LIMIT ${...}` syntax. Comparison of that commit showed no repository-source diff. Explicitly detected no-op by inspecting commit tree and red checks. Lesson: when replacement text itself contains `$`/SQL interpolation, use a replacement CALLBACK, verify the proposed bytes differ, re-fetch by exact SHA, and execute RED/GREEN checks; never trust a returned commit SHA alone.
- **Correction / GREEN:** `1b63beb6c2dbf8b5d7f939d583b9b7e794c579c0` uses callback-safe text replacement to alter ONLY the two query placeholders: `LIMIT $${values.length - 1} OFFSET $${values.length}`. Exact fixed GitHub blob `873ce9fa226cb5fcf96ca630df54bff88ecf679b` was independently fetched. The same four runtime extracted query builder cases became **4/4 PASS** with exact contiguous SQL placeholder sequences [1..N]; tenant, primary Inbox filter, prepared input types, user-selected account/keyword filters and ordering unchanged. Committed native test is source-level, and the harness uses real source code with minimal TypeScript erasure, not a hand-rewritten SQL duplicate.
- **Verification boundary:** Proven deterministic source bug and post-fix prepared SQL argument contract; **not run**: actual native `node --test apps/web/tests/mail-inbox-parameter-binding.test.mjs` in the full repo, full Vitest, web/db TypeScript, seeded PostgreSQL integration, multi-tenant role/account tests, Vercel build, production /mail/inbox or CI. No live credentials, message content, migrations, provider operations or production data touched. SQLSTATE response may persist on deployed revision until OPS-01 deploys an approved commit.
- **Prevention / next:** Dynamically constructed SQL must produce numbered `$n` placeholders for every bound argument, including LIMIT/OFFSET; test all combinations of optional account and text filters, not just a single page. In an equipped current-Development checkout execute `node --test apps/web/tests/mail-inbox-parameter-binding.test.mjs`, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web exec vitest run tests/mail-imap-folder-copies.test.ts tests/email-repository-mail-contract.test.ts`, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/db typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @ai-comms-agent/web typecheck`, and disposable PostgreSQL account/no-account Inbox pages with different limits and offsets. OPS-01 owns actual deployment, DB/network/CI and post-deployment error-rate check. Continue FIX-01 independent code defects; no gate polling and no claim live Inbox restored without evidence.

## 2026-10-24 — FIX live inbound WhatsApp total loss (SQLSTATE 42P08 + E.164 mismatch + retry skip)

- **Defect 1 (total inbound loss):** `insertWhatsAppInboundMessage` referenced parameter `$3` (`agent_id`) both as a bare `VALUES` entry and inside `CASE WHEN $3 IS NULL`. PostgreSQL cannot infer a parameter type from an `IS NULL` test, so the prepared statement failed at parse time with **SQLSTATE 42P08** (`could not determine data type of parameter $3`) and the webhook route returned **HTTP 500 for every inbound message**. Live evidence: a real customer message delivered by Meta at 2026-10-24 12:04 UTC (`whatsapp_webhook_events` row exists, contact upserted, **zero** `whatsapp_messages` rows). Reproduced with a signed probe POST against the deployed web container: HTTP 500, no row. Fix: explicit `$3::uuid` casts on both references.
- **Defect 2 (24-hour window can never open):** Meta delivers `wa_id` / `messages[].from` **without** the E.164 leading `+`, while outbound recipients are validated and stored **with** it. Inbound contacts therefore landed on a different `whatsapp_contacts` row (`919…`) than outbound recipients (`+919…`), so every customer-service-window check (`c.phone_number = recipient`, in the send-approval decision route, the readiness route, and the worker's free-form send guard) found no inbound row and free-form text was permanently `requires_template`. Fix: `normalizeE164()` in the webhook route applied to `messages[].from` and to the `contacts[].wa_id` display-name map keys.
- **Defect 3 (retries cannot heal):** the webhook route executed `if (!event.inserted) continue;` — after the event row was written, any later failure (such as defect 1) permanently lost the message because Meta retries deduplicated at event level and skipped processing. All downstream handlers are idempotent (message insert `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the provider-message unique index; status updates target the provider message id), so processing now always runs and retries self-heal.
- **Tests:** added `apps/web/tests/whatsapp-inbound-webhook-pipeline.test.ts` (4 source-contract checks: uuid casts present, E.164 normalization applied to both `from` and the contact map, duplicate-event skip removed, idempotent insert contract retained). Existing suites re-run green: `whatsapp-provider-status-scope`, `whatsapp-route-access-boundary`, `whatsapp-send-tool.spec`, `whatsapp-tool-runtime.spec`, `whatsapp-inbound-completion`; web + db `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Live verification plan (post-deploy):** re-run the signed webhook probe expecting HTTP 200 + inserted row; replay the stored 12:04 UTC event to recover the customer's real lost message on the normalized `+…` contact; remove the orphaned no-`+` contact row; then manual web-app send + approval and AI-chat send to the same recipient.


## 2026-10-24 — FIX silent empty AI replies — reasoning-model completion budget exhausted

- **Defect:** the org's Sarvam chat model is `sarvam-105b`, a reasoning-first model. Its SSE deltas carry `delta.reasoning` first and `delta.content` afterwards, and `max_completion_tokens` (org setting 1500) bounds the COMBINED completion. With the Clara hybrid RAG system prompt (~26 KB of retrieved evidence), the model spent the entire budget on reasoning (finish_reason `length`, 0 content chars), so `readSseStream` completed with ZERO yielded chunks. Downstream this surfaced as silent empty AI chat replies and as the agent WhatsApp auto-reply failing with "Generated WhatsApp text must contain 1–4096 characters".
- **Evidence:** direct provider probes — `sarvam-105b` with the runtime's exact captured request: 1863 frames, contentLen 0, reasoningLen 5496, finish `length`. Same request with `sarvam-105b-conversations` (the runtime's own default model): content 1444 chars, reasoning 0, finish `stop` within the same 1500 budget.
- **Fix (code):** `packages/clara-core/src/runtime.ts` `readSseStream` now tracks whether any `delta.content` chunk was yielded; a completion that ends with zero content throws a descriptive error ("AI provider streamed a completion with no content…") instead of completing silently. Reasoning text is deliberately NOT surfaced as chat output. Tool-call paths already fail closed.
- **Fix (live config):** org `ai_provider_configs` sarvam/chat model switched `sarvam-105b` → `sarvam-105b-conversations` on the VPS (matches the runtime's designed default and yields direct content).
- **Tests:** `packages/clara-core/tests/runtime-empty-stream-guard.test.mjs` (2 source-contract checks); full clara-core suite 41/41 pass; tsc clean.

Also exercised "Reject" twice (probe artifact + agent auto-reply proposal) and approved the AI-chat-proposed message. Screenshots saved locally.

This is a test." → agent invoked the `whatsapp.send` tool (audit: `agent.tool_call` success + `whatsapp.send_approval.requested` pending, source=agent_tool) → chat answered "WhatsApp send request is pending human review … No message was sent." → approval visible on `/whatsapp` → approved → queued → worker dispatched to Meta. Full approval-gated loop verified end to end.

  1. Free-form text send at 14:57 UTC → Meta `(#131005) Access denied`. Diagnosis: the SMTS WA account is a **Meta test phone number**; test numbers only deliver free-form text to recipients added to the test recipient list (Meta App → WhatsApp → API Setup → "To"). After both, the identical send/approve flow delivers — the 24-hour window is already open locally and at Meta until 2026-10-25 12:04 UTC.
- **Hardening recommendation (not implemented):** connected WhatsApp accounts are not periodically re-verified, so an expired token leaves the UI showing "connected" until a send fails; a scheduled re-verification loop (like `verifyPendingWhatsAppAccounts`) for connected accounts would surface token expiry proactively. The inbound agent auto-reply prompt (`whatsapp.inbound_command` task framing) produces task-summary-style replies rather than natural customer replies — a prompt-quality follow-up.

## 2026-10-24 — Autonomous sending (admin-enableable no-approval outbound) — feature + live acceptance

- **Mandate:** owner asked why AI still requires human approval for WhatsApp/Email sends, where to switch it off, and to enable + test it (implement if missing).
- **Investigation:** the human approval gate is by design — tools are literally `email.send.request` / `whatsapp.send` ("request approval", never auto-send); no org/agent/env setting to bypass it existed anywhere (searched DB schema, migrations, env, policy code).
- **Feature (commit 48ee9d5b, deployed via update-and-up.sh):**
  - Agent policy `security_policy.autonomous_send: true` waives the human approval step for that agent's own send requests. Only organization admins can save it (existing policy PATCH authorization).
  - Email: `maybeAutoApproveEmailAction` in the write tools calls the new `autoApproveEmailAction` transition — identical to a human approve except `approved_by` stays NULL (policy-consent marker) and the approvals row carries `auto-approved:agent_policy_autonomous_send`. The worker's 5s dispatch loop sends it.
  - WhatsApp: `autoApproveWhatsAppSendAction` in the tool runtime enforces the SAME Meta guardrails as the manual decision route (24-hour customer-service window for free-form text → otherwise `requires_template`; approved templates only), then approves + `queueWhatsAppOutboundMessage` + marks queued.
  - Dispatch-time authorization: `assertCurrentEmailActionAuthorization` now re-verifies the saved autonomous-send policy (fail-closed if an admin disabled it after creation) instead of a human approver's `organization.update`; requestedBy's `email.write`, account connected state and agent binding checks unchanged. WhatsApp path keeps `whatsapp.send` permission checks on requester/approver.
  - Frontend: Agent detail → Policy tab gains a prominent "Autonomous sending (no human approval step)" toggle with amber warning when ON, explicit enable/disable confirm text, and accurate post-save notification; the mail/WA setup checklists and tool descriptions now describe both modes; chat replies report the auto-approved queued state ("...auto-approved by this agent's autonomous-send policy and is queued for delivery...").
  - Tests: new `apps/worker/tests/autonomous-send-policy.spec.ts` (15 tests: flag semantics, operation-result mapping incl. forged-output fail-closed, source gating, DB transition keeps approved_by NULL, dispatch-time policy recheck, frontend toggle persistence) + `whatsapp-send-tool.spec.ts` expanded with 3 autonomous-path behavior tests (auto-approve+queue, window-closed fail-closed, policy-off never queues) + updated 2 existing specs that asserted the old never-queue contract. Full suites green: web 858/858, worker 349/349; tsc clean for shared/db/email-agent-runtime/worker/web.
- **Live acceptance on VPS (org ae005559, agent Sarvam AI 240019d9):**
  1. Policy enabled through the REAL API path (GET policy → PATCH with expectedPolicy snapshot; the first attempt hit the CAS `policy_changed` guard exactly as designed — a re-POST with a pristine snapshot succeeded).
  2. **Email autonomous send: FULLY VERIFIED END-TO-END.** Invoking the agent's real tool runtime (draft.create → send.request): tool returned `status:"auto_approved"`; worker dispatched within 5s; `email_actions` reached `completed` with a real SMTP providerMessageId; approvals row `auto-approved:agent_policy_autonomous_send`; audit `email.action.auto_approved` (source agent_policy_autonomous_send); `approved_by` NULL with the dispatch-time policy recheck passing. A real QA email was delivered from mohammedrilan20022002@gmail.com to smtscontracting@gmail.com with no human click.
  3. **WhatsApp autonomous send: pipeline fully verified up to Meta.** AI chat "Send a WhatsApp message to +919629534701…" → tool state `queued` (auto-approved), audit `whatsapp.send_approval.auto_approved`, message queued, worker claimed and dispatched — Meta rejected with `Authentication Error` because the temporary access token (pasted ~20:28 UTC, worked for the 20:43/20:45 deliveries) is dead again. Readiness probe confirms `provider_error`. Account status set to `error` with a last_error hint so the UI surfaces it; re-pasting a fresh token (WhatsApp → Edit) restores sending with the autonomous path unchanged.
  4. Default-off behavior re-verified live: with the flag unset, the identical chat prompt produced the classic `pending_approval` + "pending human review" reply.
  5. Model note (pre-existing): the Sarvam chat model reliably executes single-step tool calls (whatsapp.send) but often stops after `email.accounts.list` on multi-step email flows; the tool-level test above covers the email path deterministically. When the model does complete the chain, the same auto-approval applies.
- **Owner actions:** paste a fresh Meta access token (or move to a permanent System User token to stop the 24h expiry cycle); optionally add +919629534701 to the test number's allowed recipients for free-form text. Autonomous sending is currently ENABLED for the Sarvam AI agent; turn it off any time via Agents → Sarvam AI → Policy → Autonomous sending.

## Scheduled AI agent — merged Scheduling + Automations with full agent tool control

**Task ID:** scheduled-ai-agent-merge
**Date:** 2026-10-25
**Commits (Development):** ae7b911e, 152954fe, a51bb48b, 94e64e37 — deployed to the VPS (HEAD 94e64e37)

### What was built
1. **Merge**: `/schedules` + `/automations` now redirect to the merged **Scheduled AI agent** workspace at `/scheduled`; single sidebar item; overview/routines/whatsapp deep links updated.
2. **Agent scheduling tools** (`apps/worker/src/agent/schedules-tool-runtime.ts`): `schedules.list/create/update/delete` host-authorized tools. Recurrence presets map deterministically to validated 5-field cron: minutes (1–59), hours (1–23), daily/weekly/monthly at HH:MM, raw cron, or one-time future ISO fire; timezone validated (IANA); new `schedules.read`/`schedules.manage` permissions (owner/admin manage, member read). Tools wired into the interactive chat tool loop and the agent policy checklist UI.
3. **Scheduled executions run the full tool loop** (`scheduled-agent-runtime.ts`): at fire time the stored task instruction becomes the user turn; the agent gets its REAL email + WhatsApp tools, least-privilege permissions derived from its saved policy, and send/audit attribution to the schedule creator (`created_by`, now carried through `claimDueScheduledJobs`). Legacy intents keep the prompt-only dispatcher. Autonomous-send policy applies inside scheduled runs exactly as in chat.
4. **Chat feedback**: schedules.create/update/delete map to schedule-channel OperationResults; replies confirm "Scheduled AI task created/updated/deleted" with a link to `/scheduled` instead of the no-send failure classifier text.
5. **Run-history evidence**: `GET /api/organizations/[orgId]/schedules?jobId=` returns scheduled_job_runs; the merged screen shows per-task execution history (fire time, status, tool-call chain, agent output).

### Critical production bug found and fixed (a51bb48b)
`advanceScheduledJob`'s revision CAS compared a millisecond-precision JS Date snapshot to PostgreSQL microsecond `updated_at` — exact equality matched **zero** rows. Consequence (pre-existing): recurring schedules fired exactly once and silently stopped advancing; one-time schedules never archived. Fixed with a same-millisecond range comparison; verified live (a `*/2` schedule fired at 00:30 → advanced to 00:32 → fired again → advanced to 00:34).

### Testing evidence
- **Code**: worker 366/366, web 871/871, tsc clean; 18 new worker tests + 12 new/updated specs.
- **Deploy incident handled**: VPS root disk hit 100% (Postgres crash-looping "No space left on device"); reclaimed ~64GB (build cache, dangling images, prior-session aca-repair one-offs, stopped containers); Postgres recovered automatically.
- **Terminal E2E (one-time, exact user scenario)**: schedule "Summarize my emails from the last 24 hours and send a short note on WhatsApp to +919629534701" fired on time; agent ran email.accounts.list → email.search ×2 → whatsapp.send (5 tool calls); produced a real summary of 7 emails across 2 mailboxes; WhatsApp send auto-approved by the autonomous-send policy (audit `whatsapp.send_approval.auto_approved`, approved_by NULL) and dispatched by the worker to the Meta Graph API — recorded `Authentication Error` there because the temporary Meta token is dead again (owner must re-paste; the 24h window is open and delivery itself was proven delivered in the prior session).
- **Recurring E2E**: `*/2 * * * *` schedule fired twice with next_fire_at advancing each time (repeat functionality proven after the CAS fix).
- **Frontend manual**: created recurring (*/10) and one-time schedules from the UI form; verified rows, pause→paused, resume→active, Runs history renders the tool-call chain + agent output, delete→empty state; screenshots saved.
- **AI chat E2E**: natural-language create ("Daily evening digest, every day 6:30 PM Asia/Calcutta") → `schedules.create` allow; update request → agent chained `schedules.list` → `schedules.update` (timezone → Asia/Calcutta, next fire 13:00 UTC = 18:30 IST); delete request → model self-corrected an invalid delete (invalid_input) by listing then deleting successfully; chat replies confirmed each operation. All QA schedules archived; minted sessions revoked.

### Owner actions
- Re-paste the WhatsApp access token (Meta console) or move to a permanent System User token — the only blocker between the scheduled WhatsApp note and actual delivery.

## 2026-10-25 — fix(scheduling): coherent chat feedback for self-corrected schedule deletes (e51fe75c)

### Live defect traced (user report: "1 failed message and 1 success message from AI chat")
Conversation `aca.conversation_messages` + `aca.audit_events` (2026-10-25 00:46:42–46, "Daily evening digest"):
1. 00:46:43 `schedules.delete` → **`invalid_input`** (schema validation, 1 ms — never executed). The model passed the task name, not the 36-char UUID the old schema demanded.
2. 00:46:44 model self-corrected: `schedules.list` → real UUID.
3. 00:46:45 `schedules.delete` → allow → `schedule.archived`.
4. The stored assistant reply rendered **BOTH** same-turn OperationResults → one contradictory message: "Scheduled AI task deleted failed. The action could not be confirmed. Check the account and review queue before retrying." followed by "Scheduled AI task deleted. [Review scheduled tasks](/scheduled)…".

Two code defects: (a) `formatOperationFeedback` echoed superseded pre-execution failures next to the final outcome; (b) `failureExplanation` had no schedule error-code cases, so schedule failures fell to the email/WhatsApp default ("check the account and review queue").

### Fix (e51fe75c, rebased over consent/draft-scope fixes d6131b6d)
- **Web `operation-feedback.ts`**: `collapseSupersededScheduleFailures` drops a failed schedule result only when a later same-operation call completed in the same turn AND the error code proves no mutation persisted (invalid_input, not_found, agent_mismatch, permission_denied, tool_not_allowed, context_mismatch, ambiguous_name, invalid_schedule, agent_not_found, unknown_tool, authorization_unavailable). Business failures (idempotency_conflict…) and every email/WhatsApp result are never dropped. New `scheduleFailureExplanation` gives schedule-specific guidance (points to /scheduled + schedules.list); the email/WhatsApp default text can no longer leak into schedule failures.
- **Worker `schedules-tool-runtime.ts`**: new exported `resolveScheduleReference` — `schedules.delete`/`schedules.update` accept the exact UUID **or the task's unique name** (quoted, trimmed, case-insensitive, active-only; ambiguous names fail with actionable `ambiguous_name`). Input schema relaxed from a bare 36-char UUID to 1–120 chars; tool descriptions teach the name path. This makes the natural user phrasing succeed on the first tool call.

### Verification (all four steps)
- **Code**: worker 372/372 (+6), web 877/877 (+6), tsc + eslint clean.
- **Deploy**: `update-and-up.sh` → HEAD e51fe75c; verified `resolveScheduleReference` in worker dist (0 remaining 36-char constraints) and the collapse logic in the built web chunks; containers healthy.
- **Live AI chat regression (dedicated QA conversation, minted session, both revoked)**: turn 1 create "Daily evening digest" daily 18:30 Asia/Calcutta → single clean "Scheduled AI task created." reply (cron `30 18 * * *`, tz Asia/Calcutta); turn 2 delete with the user's exact phrasing → **single** "Scheduled AI task deleted. [Review scheduled tasks](/scheduled)…" reply, `schedules.delete` **allow on the first call** (name resolution, no invalid_input, no retry loop), job archived. Turn 3 delete of a nonexistent name → model lists and answers honestly ("There are no active scheduled tasks…"), zero email/WhatsApp wording. Frontend smoke: /scheduled HTTP 200, 0 active schedules.
- Harnesses persisted: `scripts/ssh-bridge/sched-delete-regression-{a,b}.sh`, `delete-fix-{deploy,verify*,codecheck}.sh`.
